## 2026-10-09 (later)

### ⚠ DEPLOYED: `sks_leads` UPDATE policy extended so an appointment's assignee can complete it — applied directly to Supabase
**Commit:** Uncommitted (DB change already live)
The long-pending fix for "Complete"/"Mark Site Visit Done" silently no-op'ing for Mohammed Fazil (role `designer`, personally assigned to the lead's appointment but not the lead itself). Confirmed explicitly by the user ("Yes, apply the SQL now") after being asked twice; applied directly against the live Supabase project via `npx supabase db query --db-url ... --file ...` (verified connectivity and the pre-change policy text matched the repo first, then applied, then re-verified).
- **DB change (already applied, not a pending migration):** `"authenticated update leads"` policy on `sks_leads` — both `USING` and `WITH CHECK` gained: `OR (status IN ('qualified', 'site_visit_scheduled') AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM sks_appointments a WHERE a.lead_id = id AND a.data->'assignedToIds' ? sks_my_id()))`. Lets whoever is personally assigned to a lead's own appointment advance it through those two stages, regardless of their role — previously only owner/project_coordinator/site_engineer(at site_visit_scheduled)/designer(at internally_approved/design_in_progress)/accounts(at client_approved) could write to a lead at all.
- **Changed:** `supabase/rls-policies.sql` — updated to match what's now live, so the repo doesn't silently drift from the deployed policy.
- **Deliberately not changed:** `canCompleteSiteVisit` in `src/screens/Leads/LeadStatusSheet.tsx` stays Lead-assignee/MD-only (reverted two commits ago) — "Mark Site Visit Done" is a separate, already-confirmed product decision (the Lead stays with its owner/PC for that step), not a technical limitation anymore now that the DB permits it too.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (75) and `npm run build` all clean (no app code needed to change for this fix — it was purely a database policy gap).

### Fixed: newly-assigned appointments/tasks didn't appear in My Tasks until the page was left and reopened
**Commit:** Uncommitted
Reported: MD assigned a lead/task to Raj Kamal, and it didn't show up in his My Tasks until he navigated away and back. Root cause: `MyTaskScreen.tsx` loads `leads` from `CRMContext` (which already polls every 30s, fixed earlier this session), but loads everything else it needs — projects, tasks, MRFs, appointments, site visits, quotations, design packages — itself, in a `useEffect(() => {...}, [])` that only ever ran once on mount. Any assignment reflected in one of those other sources just sat stale until the component remounted.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/MyTasks/MyTaskScreen.tsx` — the same data load now also re-runs every 30s in the background (no loading spinner on the background refreshes, only the first load), matching the exact interval `CRMContext.tsx` already uses for leads.
- No database/schema change.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (75) and `npm run build` all clean.

### Fixed: "Submit Reschedule" silently did nothing for Mohammed Fazil — same upsert/RLS gap, now closed at the source
**Commit:** Uncommitted
Reported live: a Designer assigned to an appointment clicked "Submit Reschedule" on the Lead page and nothing happened. Same root cause flagged (but not yet fixed) two commits ago — `appointmentService.ts`'s only write function, `upsertAppointment()`, requires `sks_appointments`' INSERT policy (owner/admin/project_coordinator only) because Supabase's `.upsert()` is an INSERT…ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE under the hood. `LeadStatusSheet.tsx`'s `submitReschedule()` and both functions in `appointmentActions.ts` (`updateAppointmentStatus`, `decideAppointmentReschedule`) all write through it directly, so any of them could silently no-op (0 rows matched, no error) for a non-owner/admin/PC user — this time it was the reschedule-request flow specifically, after the "Complete" button's own gating was fixed.
- **Changed:** `src/services/appointmentService.ts` — added `updateAppointment()`, a true `.update().eq('id', ...)` call (no INSERT policy involved), same fix as `designService.ts`'s `updateDesignPackage()` from two commits ago. `upsertAppointment()` kept as-is for genuine appointment creation (`AppointmentsScreen.tsx`'s "New Appointment", still owner/PC-only by design).
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Appointments/appointmentActions.ts` and `src/screens/Leads/LeadStatusSheet.tsx` — all three status/reschedule-write call sites now use `updateAppointment()` instead of `upsertAppointment()`. `AppointmentsScreen.tsx`'s own "Edit" form (a genuine existing-row update too, but only ever reached by `edit_appointment` holders who already have INSERT rights) was left on `upsertAppointment()` — not reported broken, out of scope for this fix.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Appointments/appointmentActions.test.ts` — mock updated to match (`updateAppointment` instead of `upsertAppointment`).
- No database/schema/RLS change — pure application-layer fix, same pattern as the Design Package one.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (75) and `npm run build` all clean.

### DB issue reported, awaiting approval — not yet changed: "Complete" on a Qualified lead silently does nothing for an appointment-assigned Designer
Mohammed Fazil (role `designer`) can now reach the Complete/Rescheduled buttons (previous fix), but clicking "Complete" has no effect: `sks_leads`' UPDATE RLS policy only allows `designer` to update a lead when `status IN ('internally_approved', 'design_in_progress')` — `'qualified'` isn't covered, so the UPDATE matches 0 rows under RLS and silently no-ops (no error surfaces, which is why it looked like "the button doesn't work"). This is a genuine database-policy gap, not fixable from application code, so per the standing DB-safety rule I've reported the exact proposed SQL to the user and am waiting for explicit approval before touching `supabase/rls-policies.sql` or the live policy:
```sql
OR (status = 'qualified' AND EXISTS (
  SELECT 1 FROM sks_appointments a
  WHERE a.lead_id = sks_leads.id
  AND a.data->'assignedToIds' ? sks_my_id()::text
))
```
(added to both `USING` and `WITH CHECK` of `"authenticated update leads"`, scoped to the specific appointment assignee rather than a blanket role grant).

### Fixed: PC could never actually Approve/Reject a Design Package — same upsert/RLS gap as the appointments bug, pre-existing
**Commit:** Uncommitted
Reported via the Design Center's own "Cobra Package" review sheet (status Pending PC Review, PC Approve/Reject visible) — this is the same class of bug just found and fixed for Appointments, independently confirmed by checking `supabase/rls-policies.sql`: `sks_design_packages`' INSERT policy is `sks_has_role('owner') OR sks_has_role('designer')` only — `project_coordinator` isn't in it. Since `changeDesignStatus()` (the one shared function both `DesignCenterScreen.tsx`'s own detail sheet and the Lead-page's `DesignStatusCard` call) wrote through `upsertDesignPackage()` → Supabase `.upsert()`, and Postgres RLS evaluates the INSERT policy for any upsert regardless of whether the row already exists, a PC clicking "PC Approve" on an existing package was being rejected — on both surfaces, not something introduced this session.
- **Changed:** `src/services/designService.ts` — added `updateDesignPackage()`, a true `.update().eq('id', ...)` call (no INSERT policy involved), alongside the existing `upsertDesignPackage()` (kept for genuine package creation, still owner/designer-only by design).
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Design/designActions.ts` — `changeDesignStatus()` now calls `updateDesignPackage()` instead of `upsertDesignPackage()`. Fixes PC/MD Approve/Reject/Resubmit on both `DesignCenterScreen.tsx` and the Lead page's `DesignStatusCard` in one place, since both already route through this shared function.
- No database/schema/RLS change — pure application-layer fix.
- **Flagging, not changed:** three other call sites still write to `sks_design_packages` via the INSERT-requiring `upsertDesignPackage()` even though they're only ever editing an existing package — `CRMContext.tsx`'s `permanentlyDeleteLead()` (detaching a package from a deleted lead), `ProjectsScreen.tsx`'s project-save (attaching a package to a new project), and `ApprovalReviewSheets.tsx`'s `decideDesign()` (MD-only, so not actually broken today, but same latent risk if that ever changes). Worth the same `updateDesignPackage()` swap if a non-owner/designer role hits one of these.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (75) and `npm run build` all clean.

### Fixed (follow-up): My Tasks' inline Appointment fix from the previous commit hit an RLS error instead
**Commit:** Uncommitted
The immediately-previous fix let Mohammed Fazil open an inline "Mark Complete/Cancel" sheet from My Tasks, but clicking "Mark Complete" failed with `new row violates row-level security policy for table "sks_appointments"`.
- Root cause: `updateAppointmentStatus()` (and every appointment write in the app) goes through `appointmentService.ts`'s single `upsertAppointment()`, which calls Supabase's `.upsert()` — a SQL `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE`. Postgres RLS evaluates the **INSERT** policy for that statement even when it resolves to the UPDATE branch on an existing row. `sks_appointments`' INSERT policy is `sks_has_role('owner') OR sks_has_role('admin') OR sks_has_role('project_coordinator')` — `designer` isn't in that list, so the write was rejected even though the UPDATE policy itself (`USING (true) WITH CHECK (true)`) is fully open. This is a real, pre-existing gap in the already-shipped "assignee can Mark Complete/Cancel their own appointment" feature on `AppointmentsScreen.tsx` too — it just hadn't been hit by a non-PC/MD/owner assignee until now.
- The user clarified the actually-correct flow: completing an appointment is a **Lead-level** action — `LeadStatusSheet`'s `'qualified'` case already has working Complete/Rescheduled buttons that advance `Lead.status` to `'site_visit_scheduled'` (via `updateLead`, not a direct `sks_appointments` write at all) — exactly the same pattern Site Visit completion already uses. No new DB/RLS change needed; the fix is to stop writing to `sks_appointments` directly from My Tasks.
- **Reverted:** `src/screens/MyTasks/MyTaskScreen.tsx` — removed the inline Mark Complete/Cancel sheet, its state, and the `updateAppointmentStatus` call added in the previous commit.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/MyTasks/myTaskFeed.ts` — the "Appointment Today" item now uses `leadAction: { leadId }` (same as the Site Visit item right below it) instead of the just-added `appointmentAction`, so "Update Status" opens `LeadStatusSheet` inline on the Lead's own `'qualified'` Complete/Reschedule flow. Removed the now-unused `MyTaskItem.appointmentAction` field.
- No database/schema/RLS change made. **Flagging, not fixed:** the underlying `upsertAppointment()`-always-needs-INSERT-rights gap is still live in `AppointmentsScreen.tsx`'s own "Mark Complete/Cancel" card for any assignee outside owner/admin/PC — worth a real fix (e.g. a true `.update()` path for status-only changes, or widening the INSERT policy) if that's hit again elsewhere.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (75) and `npm run build` all clean.

### Fixed: Lead page's "Client Approved" stage jumped straight to "Mark Won → Create Project" after the advance decision, skipping Design
**Commit:** Uncommitted
Immediate follow-up to the previous fix — once the Advance decision correctly moved there, the very next step ("Mark Won → Create Project") appeared right after, with no step in between to actually start or finish Design. Correct flow, confirmed by the user: advance decision → Design Center → only once the client has approved the design is the lead ready to convert to a project.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Leads/LeadStatusSheet.tsx` — `case 'client_approved'`, once the advance decision is made, now branches on the real Design state: no `designPackage` yet → "Go to Design Center →"; a design package exists but isn't `client_approved` → inline `DesignStatusCard` (or a read-only reflection + "Review in Design Center →" for whoever can't act), same pattern already used for `case 'design_in_progress'`; only once `designPackage.status === 'client_approved'` does "Mark Won → Create Project" appear.
- Root cause note: `Lead.status` stays `'client_approved'` forever once a human sets it — it's intentionally outside `LEAD_STATUS_ORDER`, so the self-healing resync (`impliedMinLeadStatus`) never advances it to `design_in_progress`/`design_approved` the way it does earlier stages. That means every later real-world state (no design / design in progress / design approved) has to be handled inside this one `case` block rather than relying on separate `case`s for those statuses, same constraint that shaped the `internally_approved` fix two commits ago.
- No database/schema change.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (75) and `npm run build` all clean.

### Fixed: a lead MD assigned to someone else didn't show up in their My Tasks until they manually reloaded
**Commit:** Uncommitted
Reported: MD assigns a lead/task to Raj Kamal from their own session; Raj Kamal's My Tasks page (already open, logged in separately) doesn't show it until he refreshes the page.

**Root cause:** `CRMContext.tsx`'s `leads` — the single shared source every screen reads from, including My Tasks' "Lead: ..." cards — only ever fetched once per session (on auth-ready / user change), never again. Reassigning a lead from a *different* browser session has no way to reach an already-open tab without either a live subscription or periodic polling; this app has neither for leads, so the assignee's tab kept showing stale data indefinitely until a manual reload forced a fresh fetch.
- **Changed:** `src/context/CRMContext.tsx` — added a 30s polling interval alongside the existing fetch-on-auth-ready, matching the interval `NotificationBell.tsx` already polls notifications at (same pattern, not a new one). A reassignment now shows up in the assignee's My Tasks (and everywhere else `leads` feeds) within 30 seconds, no manual reload needed.
- No database/schema change — this is a client-side refetch cadence only, reading the same `sks_leads` table the same way `refreshLeads()` already did.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (75) and `npm run build` all clean.
- **Not independently verifiable from code alone (no browser access this session):** the actual cross-session timing — please confirm a reassignment from one account shows up in the other account's My Tasks within ~30s without a reload.

### Notification system, part 2: Mark as Read / Mark All as Read, deep-linked PC notifications, and Project Task assignment was never notifying anyone
**Commit:** Uncommitted
Follow-up to the earlier same-day notification audit. This pass covers what was still genuinely missing, found by re-checking every workflow against the request rather than assuming the previous pass covered everything.

**1. Mark as Read / Mark All as Read (new — didn't exist before).**
- `src/services/notificationService.ts` — new `markAllNotificationsRead()`. No RLS change needed: the existing UPDATE policy already scopes to `user_id = sks_my_id()`, so a bulk update with no `WHERE` clause from the client still only ever touches the caller's own rows.
- `src/components/layout/NotificationBell.tsx` — header gets a "Mark all read" action (shown only when there's something unread); each unread row gets its own small checkmark "Mark as read" control that marks it read without navigating (separate from clicking the row itself, which still opens the record **and** marks it read, as before). Restructured the row from `<button>` to `<div role="button">` so the inner mark-read `<button>` isn't invalid nested-button markup.

**2. PC-pending Quotation/Design notifications now deep-link to the exact record, not just the list.**
`/approval` is owner-only — a PC recipient clicking a "Quotation/Design Pending PC Review" notification that linked to `/approval` would hit a permission wall. These always linked to the bare `/quotations`/`/design` list instead, requiring PC to find the right one manually.
- `src/screens/Quotations/QuotationsScreen.tsx`, `src/screens/Design/DesignCenterScreen.tsx` — their existing `?quotationId=`/`state.quotationId`-style deep-link effects now also read the id from the URL's query string (`?quotationId=...` / `?packageId=...`), since a notification's `url` is a plain link and can't carry router state the way the Approval page's "Review" button does.
- `src/screens/Quotations/quotationActions.ts`, `src/screens/Quotations/QuotationsScreen.tsx`, `src/screens/Design/designActions.ts` — PC-pending and designer-revision notifications now link to `/quotations?quotationId=<id>` / `/design?packageId=<id>`, opening that exact record's Detail sheet directly.

**3. Project Task assignment never notified anyone — found by checking every "assign" code path, not just the Lead/Appointment ones already covered.**
- `src/screens/ProjectWorkspace/ProjectWorkspaceScreen.tsx` (`handleAddTask`), `src/screens/ProjectWorkspace/TaskDetailScreen.tsx` (`handleAddSubtask`, `handleReassign`) — creating a task/subtask with an assignee, or reassigning one, now notifies that person (skipped if they assigned it to themselves), linking to `/projects/:id/tasks/:taskId` — the same destination `myTaskFeed.ts` already uses for this entity.

No database/schema/RLS changes — the table and its per-user RLS policy already supported everything here.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (75) and `npm run build` all clean.

### Fixed: approving a rescheduled appointment silently skipped straight to Site Visit
**Commit:** Uncommitted
Reported via screenshots: approving a reschedule request immediately advanced the lead to "Site Visit Scheduled" and removed the Complete/Rescheduled choice — the user never got to decide what happens next.

**Root cause:** `decideRescheduleAndAdvanceLead()` (`src/screens/Appointments/appointmentActions.ts`), the single function both the central Approval page and the Lead page's own reschedule flow call, had `const advance = approved && lead.status === 'qualified'` and wrote `{ status: 'site_visit_scheduled' }` straight onto the Lead whenever MD approved. An accepted reschedule should only confirm the appointment's new date — it's not the same thing as the site visit actually happening.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Appointments/appointmentActions.ts` — `decideRescheduleAndAdvanceLead()` no longer touches `lead.status` on approve or reject, either way — it now only updates the appointment and logs an activity entry. Both existing callers (the central `/approval` page and the Lead page) automatically get the fix since there's exactly one implementation.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Leads/LeadStatusSheet.tsx` — the Lead's own "Pending MD Approval" section (previously just a read-only summary + a "Review in Approval →" link for owner) now shows the requester, reason, and requested date, plus — when the logged-in user is MD — inline Approve/Reject buttons (calling the same shared function) and a notes field, with "Open in Approval →" kept alongside. Non-MD users still see read-only pending status only, no action. Because lead.status no longer changes on approval, the Complete/Rescheduled choice reappears automatically once the sheet re-renders — no special-case logic needed for that part.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/MDTasks/ApprovalSection.tsx` — corrected the Approve card's description text, which still said "moves this lead to Site Visit" (now stale).
- **Added:** 2 regression tests in `src/screens/Appointments/appointmentActions.test.ts` asserting `updateLead` is never called with a `status` key, approved or rejected.
- No database/schema change — the Lead page's reschedule approval reuses the exact same `sks_appointments`/activity-log writes the central Approval page already makes; both stay in sync because they're now provably the same function call, not two copies.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (75) and `npm run build` all clean.

### Notification system audit: infrastructure already exists end-to-end — fixed 7 notifications that weren't clickable
**Commit:** Uncommitted
Requested as a full notification system build. Investigated first (per the DB-safety instruction to inspect before changing anything) and found the entire system already built and wired in: `sks_notifications` table with RLS scoping every row to `user_id = sks_my_id()` (no client-side filtering needed, confirmed in `src/services/notificationService.ts`), `NotificationBell.tsx` with an unread-count badge, a dropdown panel (title/body/timestamp, unread visually distinct), click-to-mark-read, 30s polling, and full persistence across refresh/re-login (it reads straight from the table, nothing client-cached). `sendNotification()` (`src/utils/notifications.ts`) is already the single call every workflow uses — MRF, Project Stage, Product Sale, Leave, Lead reassignment, Appointment assignment, Quotation PC/MD, Design PC/MD, and Design revision all already call it on the right event, to the right recipients (role- or assignment-scoped, never broadcast).

**The actual gap:** 7 of those calls were missing their 4th argument (the click-through `url`), so the notification fired and appeared in the bell correctly, but clicking it did nothing (`NotificationBell.tsx`'s `handleOpen()` only navigates `if (n.url)`). Added the missing links:
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Leads/LeadStatusSheet.tsx` — "Appointment Rescheduled" (sent to every MD when a reschedule is requested) now links to `/approval`. This was the most significant gap — it's the exact Rescheduled-Appointment MD-approval case you named explicitly, and it had no link at all.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Quotations/quotationActions.ts`, `src/screens/Quotations/QuotationsScreen.tsx` (the older, still-live duplicate pair — see note below) — "Quotation Pending MD Approval" → `/approval`, "Quotation Pending PC Review" → `/quotations`.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Design/designActions.ts` — "Design Pending MD Approval" → `/approval`, "Design Pending PC Review" → `/design`, "Design Sent Back for Revision" → `/design`.
- **Pre-existing duplication flagged, not fixed:** `QuotationsScreen.tsx` still has its own local `notifyMdPending`/`notifyPcPending` (used by `handleAddQuotation`/`handleUpdateQuotation`) alongside the newer versions in `quotationActions.ts` (used by `handleStatusChange`) — both now correctly linked, but this is the kind of near-duplicate the instructions warned against. Didn't consolidate it this round since it's out of scope for a notification-links fix and touching it risks the already-verified status-change logic; flagging for a future pass.

**Database safety:** no schema/RLS change needed or made — the table and its per-user RLS policy already fully support everything asked (specific recipients only, persists across refresh/re-login, no duplicate-system risk since every workflow already shares the one `sendNotification()` entry point).
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (73) and `npm run build` all clean.
- **Not independently verifiable from code alone (no browser access this session):** that each notification's unread badge/count updates live and that no duplicate notification fires on a retried action — the UI already guards most of these triggers with `disabled={saving}` during the save, but please double-check by clicking through the scenarios in your own testing list.

## 2026-10-08

### Fixed: Lead page could get permanently stuck on a stale stage despite real Quotation/Design progress
**Commit:** Uncommitted
Reported via screenshots: a Lead's "Update Status" sheet still showed "Quotation Drafted" even though its real Quotation had gone all the way to Client Approved (with advance payment recorded) and its real Design package was already MD Approved. This made the Lead page show no useful action — not because anything was "blocked" by design, but because the Lead's `status` field had desynced from the real Quotation/Design records it's supposed to track.

**Root cause:** the auto-advance added earlier this session (`changeQuotationStatus`/`changeDesignStatus` nudging `lead.status` forward) only fires on the exact live transition, and only if the Lead's status at that moment matches the expected "previous" stage precisely. If that chain ever breaks once — a transition made before this existed, a stale read, directly-edited data — every later link silently stops firing too, since each one's own precondition never gets met, and the Lead is stuck forever with no way to catch up.

**Fix — a self-healing resync, not just a one-shot trigger:**
- `src/screens/Leads/LeadStatusSheet.tsx` — new `impliedMinLeadStatus(quotation, designPackage)`: computes the Lead's true minimum stage directly from the real Quotation/Design status (Design's progress wins over Quotation's once Design exists, since Design is expected to follow quotation approval in this pipeline; a quotation that reaches the client with *no* Design involved correctly implies `sent_to_client` on its own). Run every time the Lead sheet opens, right after fetching the real quotation/design — if the Lead is behind, it's caught up immediately (logged to activity as "Synced to actual progress: ..."). Deliberately never touches `negotiating`/`client_approved`/`won`/`lost` — those stay human decisions, never auto-set.
- New `src/screens/Leads/LeadStatusSheet.test.ts` — 8 cases covering the sync logic (no records, quotation-only completion, quotation+design together, design-progress precedence).
- No database/schema change — reads records that were already being saved correctly; this only fixes how the Lead's own status catches up to them.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (72) and `npm run build` all clean.
- **Not addressed in this change, flagging explicitly:** a true "Design without Quotation" path (your second test flow, `Requirement → Design → PC → MD → Client → Project` with no quotation at all) still isn't possible — Design can only start once the Lead reaches `internally_approved`, which still requires the Quotation to be MD-approved first (`DesignCenterScreen.tsx`'s `eligibleQuotationFor()` gate). That's a pre-existing constraint from the original Quotation/Design build, not something this fix touches — let me know if you want that as a separate follow-up, since it's a real FSM branch, not a sync bug.

### Lead-centric workflow overhaul, Phases 0-4: My Tasks deep-links, Appointment/Site Visit assignment gating, Quotation Lead-bypass fix, shared compact status panels
**Commit:** Uncommitted
A large, explicit 8-part spec (full details in your message) — planned via a 6-phase plan (saved, approved before starting). This entry covers Phases 0-4; Phase 5 (the Lead-page dashboard shell) follows in a separate entry once complete. No database/schema/RLS changes — one additive JSONB field (`Appointment.statusHistory`) plus `SiteVisitRoom.calculatedTotal`, both optional, same pattern as every other `statusHistory`/`calculatedTotal` field already in this codebase.

**Phase 0 — My Tasks can now act inline instead of only navigating away.**
- `src/screens/MyTasks/myTaskFeed.ts` — `MyTaskItem` gained `linkToState?` (router state to carry so the destination screen's own deep-link effect opens the exact record) and `leadAction?: { leadId }` (for items whose real action is a Lead-status change, not a dedicated screen).
- `src/screens/MyTasks/MyTaskScreen.tsx` — "Update Status" now opens `LeadStatusSheet` inline for `leadAction` items, or navigates with state for everything else.

**Phase 1 — Appointments: real assignee gating + history (previously: none existed at all).**
- `src/types/index.ts` — new `Appointment.statusHistory?: AppointmentStatusHistoryItem[]`.
- `src/screens/Appointments/appointmentActions.ts` — new `updateAppointmentStatus()`, the single place a manual status change happens (status write + history entry).
- `src/screens/Appointments/AppointmentsScreen.tsx` — `canActOnAppointment()` gates a new "Mark Complete/Cancel" action in the Detail sheet to the real `assignedToIds` membership (or owner) — previously only the broad `edit_appointment` role permission gated any status change, letting any PC act on any appointment regardless of real assignment. Non-actors now see "Waiting on {names}". Cards got their own "Update Status" button (opens the same sheet, no navigation). The already-existing-but-dead `state.appointmentId` deep link now actually receives traffic from My Tasks.
- `src/screens/Appointments/appointmentActions.test.ts` — 2 new tests for `updateAppointmentStatus`.

**Phase 2 — Site Visits: card-level Update Status + L×B×H.**
- `src/types/index.ts` — new `SiteVisitRoom.calculatedTotal?: string`.
- `src/screens/SiteVisits/SiteVisitsScreen.tsx` — new `calcRoomTotal()` (mirrors `RequirementsScreen.tsx`'s `calcLineItemTotal()` exactly), wired into the L/W/H inputs and shown live in both the form and Detail view. Cards got an "Update Status" button opening `LeadStatusSheet` inline for that visit's lead — completion was already correctly a Lead-level action (`canCompleteSiteVisit`, fixed earlier this session); this just makes it reachable without first going to the Leads screen.

**Phase 3 — Quotation Lead-bypass fix (the exact same bug Design had, fixed earlier this session).**
Root cause found by direct code read: `LeadStatusSheet.tsx`'s `case 'quotation_drafted'` had its own "Approve/Send Back for Rework" buttons touching only `Lead.status`, and — worse than the Design case — `submitQuotationDrafted()` was setting the lead straight to `'internally_approved'` on quotation creation, skipping the real approval stage entirely and leaving `quotation_drafted`'s own Approve/Reject buttons unreachable dead code.
- `src/screens/Quotations/QuotationsScreen.tsx` — exported `QUOTATION_STATUS_LABEL`/`quotationWorkflowInfo`; `handleStatusChange()` now advances `lead.status` to `'internally_approved'` only when the quotation truly reaches that status via MD approval.
- `src/screens/Leads/LeadStatusSheet.tsx` — `submitQuotationDrafted()` now correctly sets `'quotation_drafted'` (not `'internally_approved'`); the broken Approve/Reject UI replaced with a read-only reflection of the real `Quotation.status` + "Review in Quotations →", same shape as the already-shipped Design fix. Removed now-fully-unused `rejectReason`/`showReject` state.

**Phase 4 — Shared compact status panels, so Quotation/Design "Update Status" works on the Lead page itself.**
Per the plan's explicit design decision: extracted the *transition logic* (not the JSX) out of each screen's `handleStatusChange`, so there is exactly one implementation of the PC/MD approve/reject/resubmit + history + notify + Lead-advance behavior, reused by both the full screen and a new compact card.
- New `src/screens/Quotations/quotationActions.ts` (`changeQuotationStatus()`) and `src/screens/Design/designActions.ts` (`changeDesignStatus()`) — both screens' `handleStatusChange()` now delegate to these, keeping only screen-specific concerns locally (project contractValue sync, the legacy audit log).
- New `src/screens/Quotations/QuotationStatusCard.tsx` and `src/screens/Design/DesignStatusCard.tsx` — compact status/responsible/next + Approve/Reject/Resubmit cards.
- `src/screens/Leads/LeadStatusSheet.tsx` — the `quotation_drafted`/`design_in_progress` cases now render these cards inline (no navigation) for whoever holds the matching approval permission; everyone else still sees the read-only reflection + link to the full screen, since editing line items/files is genuinely detailed work that belongs on the dedicated screen.

### Design Revision workflow fixed: Lead screen's "Approve Design / Request Revision" was a disconnected duplicate of the real QS → PC → MD chain
**Commit:** Uncommitted
Reported via screenshots: clicking "Request Revision" / "Send Back for Revision" on the Lead's `design_in_progress` screen didn't move the workflow anywhere meaningful.

**Root cause (found by reading the code, not guessed):** `LeadStatusSheet.tsx` had its own, much older "Approve Design / Request Revision" mini-flow for the Lead's `design_in_progress`/`design_approved` stages, built before this session's QS → PC → MD approval chain existed on `DesignPackage.status`. It only ever wrote notes to the **Lead's** own status/activity history — it never touched the real `DesignPackage` record at all. This meant: (a) "Request Revision" just left a note with no actionable state change for anyone, and (b) worse, "Approve Design" let any Project Coordinator or the owner instantly flip the Lead to `design_approved` → `sent_to_client`, completely bypassing the real PC/MD approval chain that already existed correctly on the Design Center screen — a real violation of "Client must never receive the Design before PC and MD approval."

**Fix — the Lead screen now only reflects the real `DesignPackage`, never has its own parallel approval buttons:**
- `src/screens/Design/DesignCenterScreen.tsx` — exported `DESIGN_STATUS_LABEL` and `designWorkflowInfo()` (previously local) so `LeadStatusSheet.tsx` can show the same status wording. `handleStatusChange()` now also: (1) notifies the package's actual creator (resolved by name via the managed-users directory, same name-match convention `LeadsScreen.tsx` already uses) when PC rejects it — previously no one was told a revision was needed; (2) auto-advances the linked Lead's status — `design_in_progress → design_approved` only when the package truly reaches `internally_approved` (MD approved), and `design_approved → sent_to_client` only when the package's own "Send Design to Client" action actually fires — mirroring the same "advance the Lead as a side effect of the real approval" pattern already used by `decideRescheduleAndAdvanceLead` for Rescheduled Appointments.
- `src/screens/Leads/LeadStatusSheet.tsx` — removed `submitDesignApproval()`/`submitSendToClient()` and the fake Approve/Reject buttons entirely. `design_in_progress` now shows the package's real status, responsible party, next action, and rejection reason (all already tracked on `DesignPackage.statusHistory`/`rejectionReason` — no new fields needed), with a "Review in Design Center →" link into the exact package (using the `packageId` deep link added for the Approval-page fix above). `design_approved` similarly just points to Design Center to actually send it, instead of a button that used to fake-advance the Lead.
- `src/screens/MyTasks/myTaskFeed.ts` — new section 9: a `pc_rejected` design package now shows up in its creator's own "My Tasks" as "Design Sent Back for Revision" — satisfying "QS must see the Design as Revision Required in their Task," which nothing did before.
- **No database/schema/RLS changes.** Every requirement in your spec (who requested revision, who must revise, date/time, reason, resubmission date/time, approval/rejection status) is already fully covered by the existing `DesignPackage.statusHistory[]` (each entry has `status`/`label`/`note`/`by`/`byRole`/`at`) and `rejectionReason` fields from this session's earlier QS→PC→MD build — nothing needed adding.
- **Found but NOT fixed (flagging, not touching):** `LeadStatusSheet.tsx`'s `quotation_drafted` case has the exact same bug for Quotations — an "Approve / Send Back for Rework" mini-flow that only touches `Lead.status`, completely bypassing the real Quotation QS→PC→MD chain. You only asked about Design this time, so I left it as-is, but it's the identical class of problem and probably wants the same fix.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (62) and `npm run build` all clean. Please verify live: (1) a PC rejecting a design at the PC-review step now notifies the designer and shows up in their My Tasks; (2) the Lead screen can no longer jump straight to "Design Approved"/"Sent To Client" without going through PC+MD approval in Design Center; (3) once MD approves and the design is actually sent to the client from Design Center, the Lead advances on its own.

### Fixed: "New Design Package" opened for users who can't actually create one, causing an RLS error on save
**Commit:** Uncommitted
Reported via screenshot: `new row violates row-level security policy for table "sks_design_packages"` when submitting the New Design Package form reached via a Lead's "Go to Design Center →" button.
Root cause (confirmed by reading the actual RLS policy, not guessed): `sks_design_packages`' INSERT policy only allows `owner` or `designer` roles. But `LeadStatusSheet.tsx`'s "Go to Design Center →" button is reachable by anyone who can act on the lead (`canAct = can('edit_lead') || ...`) — which includes `project_coordinator`, who has `approve_design_pc` but not `upload_design`. `DesignCenterScreen.tsx`'s `leadId` deep-link effect then unconditionally opened the New Package form for whoever landed there, regardless of permission, so a Project Coordinator (or anyone else without `upload_design`) could fill out and submit a form that was always going to be rejected by the database.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Design/DesignCenterScreen.tsx` — the `leadId` deep-link effect now checks `canUpload` (`can('upload_design')`, already computed at the top of the component) before opening the form. If the user doesn't have it, the form never opens and a snackbar explains a designer/MD needs to start the package instead.
- No database/schema change — the RLS policy itself is correct and untouched; this only stops the UI from offering an action the database was always going to refuse.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (62) and `npm run build` all clean. Please confirm live that the PC (or whichever role hit this) now sees the explanatory message instead of the RLS error, and that owner/designer can still create packages normally.

### PC-pending Quotation/Design approvals now visible on the Approval page
**Commit:** Uncommitted
Bug: after the QS→PC→MD approval chain was added, quotations/design packages sitting in `pending_pc_review` or `md_rejected` (both PC's turn) never appeared on `/approval` at all — the PC-level approve/reject UI lives inline on the Quotations/Design Center screens by design, but nothing surfaced those pending items centrally. Reported by the owner (who holds the PC-approval override) seeing "0 pending approvals" despite a Design package actually waiting.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/MDTasks/ApprovalSection.tsx` — added two new entry types (`quotation_pc`, `design_pc`) to the combined pending list, covering quotations/design packages with `status === 'pending_pc_review' || status === 'md_rejected'`. Each gets its own badge ("Quotation/Design · PC Review" or "· MD Rejected") and counts toward the page's pending total. Unlike the other entry types, these don't open this page's own review sheet (which only ever handled the MD/`internal_review` and client/`sent_to_client` stages) — their "Review" button navigates straight to `/quotations` or `/design` with `{ state: { quotationId } }` / `{ state: { packageId } }`, landing on the exact record where the real inline PC approve/reject UI already works.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Quotations/QuotationsScreen.tsx` — added a `quotationId` deep-link `useEffect` (mirrors the existing `appointmentId` pattern in `AppointmentsScreen.tsx`): finds the quotation by id and opens its Detail sheet, then clears the nav state.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Design/DesignCenterScreen.tsx` — same deep-link pattern, `packageId` → opens that design package's Detail sheet.
- No database/schema change — purely additive UI wiring over existing `status` values.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (62) and `npm run build` all clean.

### Quotation workflow overhaul — calculation order fix, Transport Cost, per-section Notes, QS→PC→MD approval chain (Quotation + Design), Advance Payment Terms
**Commit:** Uncommitted
A large, multi-part change confirmed with you in two rounds of clarifying questions before starting (scope: both Quotation and Design, in one pass; Advance Payment Terms as a new field separate from the existing post-approval advance tracker). No database/schema changes anywhere in this — every new field is additive to the existing JSONB `data` columns, and the two new statuses are just new string values in the existing `status` field.

**1. Calculation order fixed: Subtotal → Discount → Taxable Amount → Tax → Transport → Net.**
- `src/screens/Quotations/QuotationsScreen.tsx` — `computeTotals()` rewritten. Previously discount was subtracted *after* tax (tax calculated on the full pre-discount subtotal); now discount is applied first, and tax is calculated only on the discounted (taxable) amount — matching your ₹1,00,000 → ₹10,000 discount → tax on ₹90,000 example exactly. Since tax is still entered per line item (`taxPercent`), a flat/percentage discount is applied proportionally across items so each item's own tax rate still applies correctly to its share — this generalizes your example correctly even when line items have different tax rates.
- The Subtotal → Discount → Taxable Amount → Tax → Transport → Net breakdown is now shown in that order in the Detail view, the Print/PDF view, the New/Edit form's live preview, and the Excel export.

**2. Transport Cost.**
- `Quotation.transportCost?: number` (new, optional). A new form field; included in `computeTotals()`'s Net Amount; shown in the Detail view, Print/PDF, and Excel export; saved with every create/edit.

**3. Per-section Notes.**
- `BOQSection.notes?: string` (new, optional — distinct from the pre-existing, already-unused `BOQLineItem.notes`). Each room/section in the New/Edit form now has its own Notes textarea below its line items — not one shared field. Shown in the Detail view and the Print/PDF view per-section.

**4. QS → PC → MD approval chain, for both Quotation and Design.**
Quotation previously went straight from creation to "Pending MD Approval." Design already had an explicit QS/designer → MD step. Both now insert a PC review step ahead of the existing, unchanged MD step:
- **New statuses** (`QuotationStatus`/`DesignPackageStatus`): `pending_pc_review` (QS/designer submitted, PC's turn), `pc_rejected` (PC rejected — back to QS/designer, reason required), `md_rejected` (MD rejected — back to **PC**, the previous responsible person, not QS — reason required). `internal_review` (MD's turn) and everything downstream of it is completely unchanged — it's now just entered via PC approval instead of directly. `draft`/`rejected` are kept only so pre-existing records in those states keep displaying correctly; nothing new writes them.
- **New permissions**: `approve_quotation_pc`/`approve_design_pc`, granted to `project_coordinator` and `owner` (as the standing override, consistent with every other approval step in this app).
- **Where the action happens**: inline on the same Quotation/Design Detail sheet the MD already uses for their own approve/reject (not a new page) — PC sees "PC Approve"/"Reject" buttons there when it's their turn, exactly like MD's existing buttons just above them in the flow.
- **Any edit after a rejection or approval now always routes back through PC** (`pending_pc_review`), never straight back to MD — so PC can never be skipped on the way back up either.
- **Discoverability**: added a new section to `src/screens/MyTasks/myTaskFeed.ts` (My Tasks) — a `project_coordinator` now sees a card for every quotation/design waiting on PC review or resubmission, linking to the real screen where the actual Approve/Reject action lives.
- **"Current Stage / Responsible Person / Next Action"**: added a compact summary card to the top of both the Quotation and Design Detail sheets, so this is never ambiguous at any stage of the whole chain (QS → PC → MD → client → advance).
- Full history (who, role, action, date/time, reason) was already recorded via the existing `statusHistory`/`approvalHistoryEntry` mechanism for every transition — the new PC steps use the exact same mechanism, no new history system.

**5. Advance Payment Terms (new, separate from the existing post-approval tracker).**
- New fields on `Quotation`: `advanceTermOption` (`with_advance`/`without_advance`/`full_advance`), `advanceTermType`/`advanceTermValue` (the %/₹ entered), `advanceTermAmount`/`advanceTermBalance` (computed). Shown and editable on the New/Edit form, saved with the quotation, shown in the Detail view and Print/PDF/Excel export.
- Deliberately **not** merged with the existing `advanceStatus`/`advancePaymentReceived`/`advancePaymentAmount` fields (set via "Proceed with Advance"/"Proceed without Advance" *after* the client approves, gating when Design can start) — as confirmed, this is the quotation's proposed *term*, decided earlier; that other mechanism tracks whether the money actually *arrived* later. Both now exist side by side, unconnected, exactly as you asked.

**Files changed**: `src/types/index.ts`, `src/data/permissions.ts`, `src/screens/Quotations/QuotationsScreen.tsx`, `src/screens/Design/DesignCenterScreen.tsx`, `src/screens/MyTasks/MyTaskScreen.tsx`, `src/screens/MyTasks/myTaskFeed.ts`, `src/utils/quotationExport.ts`.

**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (62) and `npm run build` all clean. No browser access this session — this is a large change; please test the complete flow end-to-end: Requirement → New Quotation (check the Subtotal/Discount/Taxable/Tax/Transport/Net breakdown, Transport Cost, section Notes, Advance Payment Terms) → QS creates → PC reviews (Approve/Reject with reason) → MD reviews (Approve/Reject with reason, now returns to PC not QS) → Send to Client → client decision (Approval page, unchanged) → Advance/Payment (existing post-approval tracker, unchanged) — and the same PC→MD chain on a Design package. Also check that editing a quotation/design at any rejected/approved stage correctly routes back through PC.

### Site Visit completion — gated to the actual assignee, not any manager
**Commit:** Uncommitted
Investigated the reported bug: it traced back to two *different* fields being used as if they were the same thing. Site Visit's own "Team Members" checklist (`SiteVisit.teamMemberIds`) records who physically attended/logged that visit — it is not, and was never, the Lead's assignment. The Lead's actual current assignee (who sees it on their Task page, and who the "Waiting on…" text names) is `Lead.assignedToId`, changed only via the "Reassign" control added two turns ago. Picking Santhosh as a Team Member on the Site Visit form does not assign him the lead — he has to be explicitly reassigned for that. Separately, confirmed `save('site_visit_done', …)` has exactly one call site (the "Mark Site Visit Done" button) — nothing auto-completes it from appointment confirmation or scheduling.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Leads/LeadStatusSheet.tsx` — "Mark Site Visit Done" now requires `canCompleteSiteVisit` (the actual current assignee, or the MD) instead of the broader `canAct` (which also allows any `edit_lead` manager, e.g. another project coordinator). A SiteVisit record existing is no longer enough by itself to let the wrong person close out the stage — a manager who isn't the assignee now sees "Waiting on {assignee} to mark the Site Visit done" instead of the button.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/MyTasks/myTaskFeed.ts` — the "My Tasks" Lead card's `person` line now shows `assignedToName ?? ownerName` (the real current assignee) instead of always `ownerName`, consistent with the Lead/Approval screens.
- No database/schema change — same fields, tighter gating logic only.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (62) and `npm run build` all clean. No browser access this session — please retest with an actual reassignment: on the Testing lead, use "Reassign" (not the Site Visit form's Team Members picker) to hand it to Santhosh, confirm it then appears on Santhosh's own Task page, that Raj Kamal can no longer click "Mark Site Visit Done" (only Santhosh/MD can), and that the lead only reaches Site Visit Done after that click.

### Lead assignment routing — root-cause rewrite: no more auto-reassigning to a random role match
**Commit:** Uncommitted
Root cause of "Waiting on Santhosh" showing before Raj Kamal had done anything: **every single Lead stage transition silently auto-reassigned the lead** to "whichever managed user happens to hold a fixed role for that stage" (e.g. Site Visit → the first `site_engineer` in the directory), completely ignoring who the MD had actually assigned the lead to (`Owner: Raj Kamal`). This wasn't specific to Site Visit or to this one lead — it was the mechanism behind every stage of every lead in the CRM (`ROLE_FOR_LEAD_STATUS` + `nextResponsible()` in `LeadStatusSheet.tsx`, used by `save()` on every transition). Confirmed with you this needed a pipeline-wide fix with an explicit hand-off step, not a one-screen patch.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Leads/LeadStatusSheet.tsx` — `save()` no longer touches `assignedToId`/`assignedToName`/`assignedRole` at all; a stage transition now only ever changes `status`. The lead's "current assignee" is resolved as `assignedToId/Name` if someone was explicitly assigned, else the lead's own **Owner** (`ownerId`/`ownerName` — the MD's original "Assign To"), else genuinely nobody. "Waiting on X" now always shows that real resolved person, or **"Not Assigned"** — never a role label.
- **Added:** an explicit **"Reassign"** control, shown below the Lead Details card to whoever can currently act (the assignee, or a manager with `edit_lead`) — picks a specific person from the real managed-users directory and hands the lead to them via `submitReassign()`, which is now the *only* way `assignedToId/Name/Role` ever changes. This is the "he or MD can assign the next person" step from your spec.
- **Changed:** `can('edit_lead') || user.id === the resolved assignee` is now the single act-gate, matching the exact rule `LeadsScreen.tsx`/`LeadDetailScreen.tsx` already use elsewhere — replacing the old role-based gate ("anyone holding this stage's role can act").
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Appointments/appointmentActions.ts` — `decideRescheduleAndAdvanceLead()` (added last turn) no longer auto-picks a `site_engineer` when advancing a lead to Site Visit on reschedule approval; it now only changes `status`, same as every other transition — the lead keeps whoever it's actually assigned to.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Leads/LeadsScreen.tsx`, `src/screens/Leads/LeadDetailScreen.tsx` — their own "Responsible: …" display had the same role-label-fallback bug; now reads `assignedToName ?? ownerName ?? 'Not Assigned'`, consistent with the Lead Status sheet.
- **Removed:** `ROLE_FOR_LEAD_STATUS` (the per-stage role map) and `nextResponsible()` — both fully dead once nothing auto-assigns by role anymore; confirmed zero remaining references anywhere in the codebase before deleting.
- This only touches the **Lead** pipeline, as discussed — `ProjectStatusSheet.tsx` and `ProductSaleStatusSheet.tsx` have their own, separate, identically-named `nextResponsible()` helpers for Projects/Product Sales that were not part of this request and were left untouched.
- No database/schema change — same `Lead.assignedToId`/`assignedToName`/`assignedRole`/`ownerId`/`ownerName` fields, no new columns.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (62) and `npm run build` all clean. No browser access this session — please run the exact flow from your spec: MD assigns a lead to Raj Kamal (only Raj Kamal/MD should see it as pending through every early stage, including Site Visit); Raj Kamal completes the Site Visit (lead stays with Raj Kamal, no auto-jump); Raj Kamal or MD explicitly reassigns to Santhosh via the new "Reassign" control; only then does Santhosh see "Waiting on Santhosh" / gain the ability to act.

**Commit:** Uncommitted
You asked me to inspect why some MD approvals were appearing outside Approval. That's the exact bug behind your screenshot — Approval showed "0 pending" while a real reschedule request sat waiting on a Lead's status sheet instead.
- **Added:** `src/screens/Appointments/appointmentActions.ts` — `decideRescheduleAndAdvanceLead()`, one centralized function (Appointment update + the Lead's advance-to-Site-Visit side effect + both notifications). This same logic used to be hand-duplicated three times (`LeadStatusSheet.tsx`, `TasksScreen.tsx`'s inline mini-flow, and the old deleted `MDTasksScreen.tsx`) — now there's exactly one copy.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/MDTasks/ApprovalSection.tsx` — Rescheduled Appointment is now a real 4th entry type in the same flat pending list as Quotation/Client Approval/Designing/MRF (own badge, own card, own full review sheet with Lead/appointment details, reason, requested date, Notes, Approve/Reject, and Previous/Next through the same combined queue as everything else).
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Leads/LeadStatusSheet.tsx` — removed the owner's Accept/Reject buttons from a `qualified` lead's pending-reschedule view (and the `decideReschedule()` function that backed them). It now shows the same read-only status every other role sees, plus a "Review in Approval →" button for the owner — the decision itself happens only in Approval now.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Tasks/TasksScreen.tsx` — removed the in-page Approve/Reject mini-flow for a `qualified_pending_approval` lead (and all the state/logic that only existed for it: `handleDecideInline`, `mdNotes`, `actionSaving`, `actionError`, the `managedUsers` fetch). Tapping that lead's card now shows the same read-only reschedule details plus a "Review in Approval →" button for the owner. The rest of the pipeline dashboard is unchanged.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Appointments/appointmentActions.test.ts` — added the `supabase`/`sendNotification` mocks this file now needs (same recurring vitest-environment fix used elsewhere this session), since the new shared function pulls in `sendNotification`.
- There are now exactly as many places a reschedule can be *decided* as there are for Quotation/Designing/MRF: one — the Approval page. Viewing the Lead or the Tasks pipeline still shows the pending state; only the actual decision moved.
- No database/schema change — same `Appointment`/`Lead` fields, same `rescheduleRequestStatus`/`rescheduleHistory`, nothing new added.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (62, including the appointment-decision field-write tests) and `npm run build` all clean. No browser access this session — please confirm: a lead with a pending reschedule now shows up as a card on the Approval page (not "0 pending approvals"), Review opens a full sheet with Approve/Reject, and after deciding it disappears from Pending and appears in Approval History; also confirm the Lead's own status sheet and the Tasks pipeline card for that lead now show "Review in Approval →" instead of Accept/Reject buttons.

**Commit:** Uncommitted
The Task page (`/md-tasks`) and the Approval page (`/approval`) had grown into two separate sidebar entries doing the same job — both let the MD review/approve quotations and designs via the identical shared `ApprovalReviewSheets` component, with Task additionally showing its own copy of reschedule-request approval. Consolidated so Quotation/Designing/MRF approval lives only on the Approval page; Task is now real assigned-task work only.

Kept **Assigned Tasks** and **Done History** (the real `Task`-entity sections) unchanged. Restored a **Reschedule History** section (read-only) at the bottom — accepting/rejecting a reschedule request already happens inline on the Tasks pipeline dashboard (`/tasks`, built earlier this session), but nothing else in the app showed *past* reschedule decisions, so removing it entirely would have been a real loss of visibility, not just deduplication.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/MDTasks/ApprovalSection.tsx` (the pending-queue component shared by `ApprovalScreen.tsx`) — redesigned each pending card to the FENSTER reference layout: name + Lead Owner, an amount/count box with an "Uploaded" timestamp, a Site line (lead's city, linked to Google Maps when the lead has saved coordinates — there's no literal "site visit summary" field in our data model, so this is the closest real equivalent, not fabricated data), and a full-width "Review · Approve / Reject →" button. ### Root cause found and fixed: assigned leads were invisible to the person they were assigned to
**Commit:** Uncommitted
Confirmed against live data: lead `FGD031` ("Mohamed") has `ownerId: usr_test_designer` (Mohammed Fazil — set by the "Assign To" picker on the lead form) but `assignedToId: null`. Every non-manager visibility check in the app — the Leads list, the Tasks pipeline, the personalized Task feed, and both "Update Status" button gates — filtered on `assignedToId` only. That field isn't "who this lead is assigned to" — it's a separate, auto-computed field (`LeadStatusSheet`'s `nextResponsible()`) naming whoever currently holds the role responsible for the lead's *current workflow stage*, and it stays `null` until the lead's first status change. So a lead was invisible to its own assignee from the moment it was created until someone else first advanced its status — exactly what you reported.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Leads/LeadsScreen.tsx`, `src/screens/Tasks/TasksScreen.tsx`, `src/screens/MyTasks/myTaskFeed.ts`, `src/screens/Leads/LeadDetailScreen.tsx` — every one of these now treats a lead as "mine" if the user is either `ownerId` (what Assign To actually writes) or `assignedToId` (the current stage's auto-resolved owner), not `assignedToId` alone. Same one-line fix in five places, no shared helper introduced — kept consistent with how each file already reads this field inline.
- **Added:** `src/screens/MyTasks/myTaskFeed.test.ts` (3 tests) — locks in the fix: a lead owned-but-unassigned, a lead assigned-but-owned-by-someone-else, and a lead that's neither.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/MyTasks/MyTaskScreen.tsx` — the Task feed card's "Open" button now reads "Update Status", matching the FENSTER reference. The rest of that page (summary banner, 4 live-count cards, "Ready for Next Step" section, compact cards with badge/title/meta/person) already matched the reference layout from an earlier session — no other changes needed there.
- **Not changed (already correct):** once a lead is actually visible, "Update Status" already opens the real, dynamic `LeadStatusSheet` in-page for its exact current stage — including the colored header/Call/WhatsApp/notification work from the previous change. The bug was purely that the lead never reached that screen in the first place.
- No database/schema/RLS change — both `ownerId` and `assignedToId` already exist on every lead; this only changes which existing fields the UI checks.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (62, up from 59) and `npm run build` all clean. Confirmed root cause against live data (above). ### Leads — a Lost lead now shows Restore / Delete instead of Mark as Lost
**Commit:** Uncommitted
Opening a lead already in Lost still showed "Mark as Lost", which didn't make sense for a lead already there.

Purely additive to the existing JSONB `data` column — no migration, no schema/RLS change.
- **Changed:** `src/context/CRMContext.tsx` — `bulkDeleteLeads` (the "Mark Lost" path) now also stashes `lostFromStatus`. Added `restoreLead(id)` — sets `status` back to `lostFromStatus` (or `'new'` for a lead marked Lost before this field existed), clears `lostFromStatus`/`lostReason`, logs a "Restored to …" activity entry, and reuses the existing `updateLead` write path, so the full activity history is kept and nothing is duplicated. Added `permanentlyDeleteLead(id)` — a real, permanent row delete (with the same quotation/design-package cleanup the old hard-delete path used), only ever called from the explicit confirm dialog below.
- **Changed:** `src/services/leadService.ts` — re-added a hard-delete function, named `hardDeleteLead` (distinct from the "mark Lost" meaning `deleteLead` has everywhere else in this app now) so it's unambiguous which one actually removes the row.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Leads/LeadsScreen.tsx` — the lead detail sheet now branches on `status === 'lost'`: a Lost lead shows **Restore** (immediate, no confirmation needed — it's reversible) and **Delete** (opens a confirm dialog that explicitly warns this cannot be undone); every other lead still shows "Mark as Lost" exactly as before.
- No database schema/RLS change — `lostFromStatus` lives in the same JSONB column every other Lead field already does; permanent delete reuses the existing DELETE policy (owner-only, same as it always allowed).

### File uploads on the live site — root cause is the Hostinger upload server's CORS allowlist; error message corrected
**Commit:** Uncommitted
Uploads go to a PHP endpoint on Hostinger (`VITE_UPLOAD_URL`, source `hostinger/upload.php`), not Supabase Storage. The live server's deployed copy only allows `https://spacekrafters.com` and localhost origins; the repo copy also lists `https://spacekrafters-os.vercel.app`, so the deployed file is out of date. The browser blocks the request from the Vercel origin, which surfaces as a generic "check your internet connection" error.
- **Changed:** `src/utils/fileStorage.ts` — a failed request now logs the real error to the console and shows an accurate message ("Could not reach the upload server… blocked (often a CORS setting)…") instead of the internet-connection text. Upload behavior is otherwise unchanged.
- **Action needed (not done by me — it's a server file):** re-upload `hostinger/upload.php` from this repo to the Hostinger site so the deployed allowlist matches. If the live site is on another origin (a custom domain or a different Vercel URL), that origin must also be added to `$ALLOWED_ORIGINS`.
- No database, Supabase Storage, or RLS change.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (50) and `npm run build` all clean. Live CORS check: the deployed server returns `access-control-allow-origin: https://spacekrafters.com` for `https://spacekrafters-os.vercel.app`, and allows localhost.

### MD Appointments — fixed reschedule-approval bypass, a live timezone bug, and two missing safeguards
**Commit:** Uncommitted
Client reported "an issue" with MD → Appointments → Scheduled Appointments, with a broad checklist and no specific symptom. No browser access this session, so investigated via full code audit of the appointment flow plus live read-only production database queries — full writeup in `PRODUCTION_IMPACT_REPORT_2026-10-06_appointments.md`. Found and fixed four real, confirmed bugs:
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Appointments/AppointmentsScreen.tsx` — the Edit Appointment form let anyone with `edit_appointment` (owner **and** Project Coordinator) directly change an appointment's date/time and status with zero connection to the MD-approval reschedule flow — a genuine approval bypass. Date/Time fields are now read-only for non-owner roles in edit mode (pointing to the proper "Rescheduled" lead action instead); `'rescheduled'` removed as a manually-pickable Status option for everyone (it's a dead-end state outside the real request flow). Also added the same past-time validation the create flow already had (missing on edit) and a `saving` guard against double-submission (missing entirely — no state prevented two rapid taps from creating two appointment rows).
- **Added:** `src/screens/Appointments/appointmentDate.ts` — extracted the screen's date/time helpers into a plain, testable module, plus a new `toProperIso()` helper.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Leads/LeadStatusSheet.tsx`, `src/screens/MDTasks/MDTasksScreen.tsx` — **the real bug**: a reschedule's new date/time was stored as the bare `"YYYY-MM-DDTHH:mm"` string the date/time picker fields produce (no timezone marker), then copied straight into `scheduledAt` on MD approval with no conversion anywhere — unlike every other `scheduledAt` in the app, always stored as a proper UTC ISO string. A bare string is interpreted as local time by whoever reads it back, inconsistent across devices/server. **Confirmed live: 8 of 8 appointments that have ever had an MD-approved reschedule (100%) currently have this malformed value; 0 appointments that were never rescheduled do.** Both the request point and the approval point now run the value through `toProperIso()`.
- **Added:** `src/screens/Appointments/appointmentDate.test.ts` (8 tests) — regression coverage for the timezone fix specifically (bare string → proper ISO, idempotent on already-proper ISO, preserves the actual instant) plus the past-time/parsing helpers.
- **Not changed:** the 8 existing production records with the malformed value — reformatting existing data wasn't done without explicit sign-off; the exact one-time repair SQL is in the impact report, waiting on approval.
- No database/schema change from this fix itself.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (22, up from 14) and `npm run build` all clean. Live read-only DB queries confirmed both the bug's exact scope (above) and that no duplicate-appointment rows currently exist in production. **No browser/live-site access this session** — couldn't literally reproduce on the live UI as asked; recommend a manual pass through Schedule → MD Appointments → Refresh → Re-login → Reschedule → MD Approval once deployed, per the impact report.

### App-wide readability bump — bigger reading text, chrome/buttons/badges untouched
**Commit:** Uncommitted
You asked for bigger font sizes everywhere (page titles, section headings, labels, body/form/list text, small supporting text) while keeping the sidebar/nav, buttons, cards, badges/pills, icons, and all spacing exactly the size they are today. Investigated first (via an Explore agent) rather than guessing: this app uses Tailwind's untouched default `fontSize` scale, with sizes specified two ways — ~1,420 named-class occurrences (`text-xs`…`text-4xl`, ~90 files) that flow through Tailwind's central config, and ~349 arbitrary bracket-pixel occurrences (`text-[9px]`/`[10px]`/`[11px]`, ~75 files) that bypass it entirely. Also clarified scope with you directly: button labels and colored status badges/pills (e.g. "Won", "Hot", lead-number tags) stay fixed — only free-standing reading text grows.
- **Changed:** `tailwind.config.js` — added a `theme.extend.fontSize` override, tier-shifting each named size up to reuse the next size's existing values (`text-xs` gets what `text-sm` used to be, and so on through `text-2xl`). `text-3xl`/`text-4xl` left at Tailwind's defaults — their only real usage in this app is empty-state emoji and large stat-counter numbers, not headings. This one config file makes every named-class usage in every content screen bigger automatically, with no per-file edits.
- **Changed:** `src/index.css` — added a pin-back CSS block (plain rules, outside any `@layer`, so they automatically beat Tailwind's own `@layer utilities` regardless of specificity) that re-declares the original pre-bump size for `button`/`button *`, `.rounded-full` (the established visual signature for every status badge/pill/chip in this app), and a new `.app-chrome` marker class — so those three categories never grow even though the scale change is global.
- **Changed:** `src/components/navigation/SidebarNavigation.tsx`, `MobileMenuDrawer.tsx`, `NavigationBar.tsx` — tagged with `.app-chrome` so the pin-back applies to them. (`AppHeader.tsx`, `FloatingActionButton.tsx`, `NotificationBell.tsx` turned out to need no change — their only text is already inside a `<button>`, auto-covered, except `NotificationBell`'s notification-panel content, which is real content and was deliberately left to grow like the rest of the app.)
- **Changed:** ~75 files — a separate, smaller sweep (5 parallel background agents, same pattern as earlier component-conversion sweeps this session) for the arbitrary bracket-pixel classes, which nothing in the config can reach: `text-[8px]→[9px]`, `[9px]→[10px]`, `[10px]→[11px]`, `[11px]→[12px]`, skipping anything inside a `<button>` or a `rounded-full` badge/pill. Mid-sweep, multiple agents independently flagged the same real ambiguity — many list rows/cards in this app are implemented as a whole `<button>` wrapping a title + description/date (for tap-handling), and the literal "skip inside button" rule was wrongly also skipping that content. Resolved and applied consistently via follow-up passes to every batch: a button wrapping multiple distinct pieces of info (a row/card) has its content bumped as list/body text; only a button whose entire content is a single short action label (e.g. "Save", "Retry", a tab name) stays fixed.
- No database/schema change.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (14) and `npm run build` all clean across the whole project after every batch and every correction pass. No browser access this session — this is a large, purely visual change with no live verification possible here. Needs a real visual pass before being fully trusted: check a list screen (Leads), a form (New Project), a card-heavy screen (Dashboard), and the nav/sidebar on desktop and mobile, confirming reading text is visibly bigger while nav labels, button labels, and badge pills look unchanged, and no row/card has grown or started clipping.

### SearchableSelect/DateField/TimeField — scrolling the option list closed the whole dropdown
**Commit:** Uncommitted
A regression from the portal fix above: you showed the dropdown closing/"moving" the moment you tried to scroll through the Stage options. Root cause: `useTriggerRect`'s scroll-close listener is `window.addEventListener('scroll', handler, true)` (capture phase), so it never attached directly to the panel — intentional, to catch the *page* (the BottomSheet behind the panel) scrolling and close the panel so it doesn't visually detach from its trigger. But the native `scroll` event doesn't bubble, only captures, and a capture-phase listener on `window` sees *every* scroll in the document on its way down to the actual target — including the dropdown's own internal option list. So scrolling the list itself was being misread as "the page scrolled" and immediately closed the dropdown.
- **Changed:** `src/components/forms/useOverlayPosition.ts` — `useTriggerRect` now takes an optional `panelRef`; the scroll handler checks whether the scroll event's target is inside the panel and ignores it there, only closing for a scroll genuinely outside the panel.
- **Changed:** `SearchableSelect.tsx`, `DateField.tsx`, `TimeField.tsx` — each now passes its own `panelRef` into the hook.
- No database/schema change.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (14) and `npm run build` all clean. No browser access this session.

### SearchableSelect/DateField/TimeField — vertical viewport clipping + keyboard navigation
**Commit:** Uncommitted
Follow-up to the portal fix above: that solved the panel being overlapped by other content, but the Stage dropdown (8 options) in New Project was still getting cut off by the bottom of the browser viewport itself — the panel opened below the trigger with no regard for how much room was actually left on screen. You also asked for proper keyboard behavior so scrolling the list can never change the value and the dropdown opens in the right direction with no clipping, on both desktop and mobile.
- **Changed:** `src/components/forms/SearchableSelect.tsx` — the panel now opens **above** the trigger instead of below when there isn't enough room (same logic as the horizontal clamp, applied vertically), and its option list's max-height shrinks to whatever space is actually available in that direction instead of a fixed 224px that could run past the screen. Added `overscroll-contain` so scrolling to the end of the list doesn't also scroll the BottomSheet behind it. Added keyboard navigation: Arrow Up/Down move a highlight (visually distinct from the currently-selected value), Enter picks the highlighted entry, Escape closes — picking an option was already click/Enter-only (the list is plain buttons, not a native `<select>`, so mouse-wheel scrolling was never able to change the value; this just adds the keyboard path on top of click, as asked).
- **Changed:** `src/components/forms/DateField.tsx`, `TimeField.tsx` — same vertical open-upward flip (their panels have an internal max-height already, so no list-shrinking needed, just the flip).
- No database/schema change.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (14) and `npm run build` all clean. No browser access this session.

### Dashboard — a closed project wasn't counting as "Done" in Sales Team Performance
**Commit:** Uncommitted
You reported finishing a project today but it not showing up under "Done" on the Dashboard. `Project` has two separate fields — `stage` (detailed workflow position, 8 values ending in `'closed'`) and `status` (`active`/`on_hold`/`closed`/`cancelled`) — and `src/screens/Dashboard/salesPerformance.ts`'s "Done" bucket only checked `stage === 'closed'`, which only gets set by the full MD-approval stage-advance-to-closed workflow (`ProjectStatusSheet.tsx`). But `ProjectsScreen.tsx`'s own Edit form lets you set `status` to "Closed" directly and independently of `stage` — and `status === 'closed'` is already the signal the rest of the app treats as "fully done" (`OutstandingProjectDetailScreen.tsx`/`MyProjectDetailScreen.tsx` both already show 100% progress off `status`, not `stage`). Confirmed live: a project named "Nirmal Kumar" has `status: 'closed'` with `stage: 'execution'` — exactly this mismatch.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Dashboard/salesPerformance.ts` — the Done-bucket check now reads `project?.status === 'closed'` instead of `project?.stage === 'closed'`. This is a superset of the old check (the proper MD-approval path already sets both fields together), so nothing that used to count as Done stops counting — it just also now catches projects closed directly via the Status field. Shared by both `DashboardScreen.tsx` and `SalesPersonDetailScreen.tsx` (same source function), so both are fixed together.
- No database/schema change — read-only `SELECT` used only to confirm the diagnosis.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (14) and `npm run build` all clean, plus the live DB query above confirming the exact mismatched record. No browser access this session to visually confirm the Dashboard count updates.

### SearchableSelect/DateField/TimeField — open panels now portal to document.body
**Commit:** Uncommitted
You showed two cases of a dropdown/calendar panel being overlapped by a nearby submit button instead of rendering on top of it (New Requirement's Unit select got cut off by the gold "Capture Requirement" button; New Project's Expected Handover calendar rendered underneath "Save Project" instead of over it). Root cause: all three components' open panels were `position: absolute` nested inside a BottomSheet's `overflow-y-auto` content area — that combination makes a panel's visible stacking depend on the scrollable container's current layout/scroll state, not just its own z-index, so a plain in-flow button below it can end up painting over part of it regardless of `z-50`. The two previous fixes (compact Unit column, DateField/TimeField's `alignRight` flip) were real but only addressed horizontal overflow — this is a different, deeper issue with the positioning approach itself.
- **Added:** `src/components/forms/useOverlayPosition.ts` — small shared hook (`useTriggerRect`) that tracks a trigger element's live `getBoundingClientRect()` while its panel is open, and closes the panel on scroll (same as a native control's popup) rather than trying to track continuous repositioning.
- **Changed:** `src/components/forms/SearchableSelect.tsx`, `DateField.tsx`, `TimeField.tsx` — each component's open panel now renders via `createPortal(..., document.body)` as `position: fixed`, positioned from the trigger's live screen coordinates instead of CSS `position: absolute` relative to an in-page wrapper. This is the same approach `BottomSheet.tsx` already uses for exactly this reason. DateField/TimeField's `alignRight` flip-on-overflow logic is replaced by a direct horizontal clamp against the viewport (simpler, same effect, now also correct vertically). No visual/API changes for any of the ~30+ existing call sites.
- No database/schema change.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (14) and `npm run build` all clean. No browser access this session to confirm the exact overlap is gone — this fixes a real, understood layering bug in the right component (the shared panel-positioning mechanism, not a one-off per-screen patch), so it should resolve both reported cases and any other occurrence of the same pattern app-wide.
