## 2026-10-01

### Files tab rebuilt — single cross-app browser for every uploaded file
**Commit:** Uncommitted
You asked for a proper Files tab (search, upload, categories, original filename/type/related project-client/category/date, persisting across refresh, permission-respecting), using a reference-app screenshot for the UI. Investigated first per the standing DB-safety rule: a `/files` screen already existed but only covered one narrow feature (`ProjectDocument`/`ProjectDocumentVersion`, tables `sks_documents`/`sks_document_versions`). Also corrected an assumption in the request — this app doesn't use Supabase Storage; every upload goes through a Hostinger PHP endpoint (`hostinger/upload.php` via `storeFile()`), Supabase only stores the resulting URL string inside each record's existing JSONB column. Went through full Plan Mode (2 Explore agents + 1 Plan agent) before writing anything, given the cross-cutting scope.
- **Zero schema/database change.** Every file in the app is a bare string already living in a `string[]` field on an existing row — Site Visit room photos, Requirement files, Quotation PDFs, MRF vendor files, Design version files, Daily Diary photos, Punch List photos, Expense receipt/payment-proof files, plus the existing Project Documents feature. No new table, column, or migration anywhere in this change.
- **Added:** `src/screens/Files/fileAggregators.ts` — one pure adapter function per of the 9 categories above, flattening each entity's existing `getAllX()` records into a shared `AggregatedFile` view-model (screen-local, not a DB type), resolving each file's related Project/Client via existing relationships (preferring `Project.clientName`, falling back through Lead where there's no Project yet) and its date via the existing `getUploadedAt()` filename-timestamp helper, falling back to the parent record's own `createdAt` for files uploaded before that convention existed (never blank/crashes).
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Files/FilesScreen.tsx` — full rewrite. Search (by filename/project/client), category filter chips (All + the 9 categories), date filter chips (All/Today/Yesterday/This Week/This Month/Custom, Custom using two `DateField`s), flat reverse-chronological card list (thumbnail/name, category pill, related record + project/client, date), tap-to-preview via the existing `FilePreviewModal` (view/download/print). Upload button reuses the existing `ProjectDocument`/`ProjectDocumentVersion` mechanism exactly as `ProjectWorkspaceScreen.tsx`'s "Add Document" flow already does it (optional Project picker via `SearchableSelect`, files via `MultiFileUploadField`) — no new table or service function.
- **Permissions:** no new permission logic added. The screen calls each entity's existing `getAllX()` unchanged, so Supabase RLS (already enforced per-table — e.g. MRF reads are role-restricted, Site Visits/Requirements/Quotations are open to all authenticated users) filters results exactly as it already does on each source screen. A user sees in Files precisely what they could already see by visiting Site Visits/Quotations/MRF/etc. directly.
- **Deliberately excluded from this build** (flagged in a code comment, not silently dropped): per-transition `StatusHistoryItem.files` (Project/MRF history), `VendorBill.documentFiles` (Procurement, 3-hop join), and Attendance capture photos (HR-only, no project/client relationship). Say if you want any of these added.
- Route/nav/screen-permission wiring (`/files`, nav item, `SCREEN_PERMISSIONS['files']`) already existed and needed no change.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (14) and `npm run build` all clean. No browser access this session — once available, needs the manual checks listed in the plan (all 9 categories populate; a narrower role like `site_engineer` sees an empty MRF category while everything else still shows; search/category/date filters; Upload creates a file that survives a refresh and re-login).

### DateField/TimeField panels overflowing the viewport in right-hand-column fields
**Commit:** Uncommitted
You showed New Project's "Expected Handover" calendar (the right-hand field in the Start Date/Expected Handover 2-column row) rendering with its weekday header and day grid cut off — only 5 of 7 columns visible. Root cause: both `DateField`'s 280px panel and `TimeField`'s 180px panel always anchor `left-0` to their own trigger with a fixed width — fine for a full-width or left-column field, but when the trigger is the narrower right-hand field in a 2-col row, the panel's fixed width runs past the right edge of the viewport/BottomSheet with no way to reposition itself.
- **Changed:** `src/components/forms/DateField.tsx` / `src/components/forms/TimeField.tsx` — on open, each now measures its own trigger's position (`getBoundingClientRect`) and flips to `right-0` anchoring instead of `left-0` whenever the fixed-width panel wouldn't fit to the right of the trigger. Every existing usage is unaffected when there's room (the common case) — this only changes behavior for fields that were actually overflowing.
- No database/schema change.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (14) and `npm run build` all clean. No browser access this session.

### New Quotation line-item row — Unit dropdown misaligned, column widths overflowing 100%
**Commit:** Uncommitted
You showed the Unit `SearchableSelect` in a line item rendering as a cramped "S ⌄" with the chevron overlapping the truncated label. Two compounding causes: the five columns (`Qty/Unit/Rate/Tax%/Amount`) were sized `w-1/4 + w-1/5 + w-1/4 + w-1/5 + w-1/4`, which adds up to 115% — the row was overflowing its own container regardless of what was in it; and `SearchableSelect`'s trigger always uses the same `px-4 py-3 text-sm` padding sized for a full-width field, far bigger than the `px-2 py-1.5 text-xs` compact number inputs next to it in this row, so "sqft" had no room and fell back to "s".
- **Added:** `src/components/forms/SearchableSelect.tsx` — new optional `compact` prop; when set, the trigger uses `px-2 py-1.5 text-xs` (matching the compact inputs it sits next to) instead of the default full-size padding. Every other existing usage (31 other files) is untouched since the prop defaults off.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Quotations/QuotationsScreen.tsx` — line-item row widths corrected to `18% + 22% + 20% + 15% + 25%` (sums to 100%), Unit's `SearchableSelect` now passed `compact`, Amount's value given `truncate` so a long total can't push the row width either.
- No database/schema change.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (14) and `npm run build` all clean. No browser access this session.

### Image upload CORS fix — local dev origin `spacekrafters-ops.localhost` was never allow-listed
**Commit:** Uncommitted
You reported the New Site Visit form's photo upload failing in mobile view with "Upload failed — check your internet connection and try again." Looked the same as the earlier-diagnosed stale-Hostinger-deployment issue, but this screenshot was from a local dev server (`spacekrafters-ops.localhost:5173`), not production — so I checked the actual Origin this dev server sends. `src/utils/fileStorage.ts` always posts to the live Hostinger endpoint (`VITE_UPLOAD_URL=https://spacekrafters.com/os/upload.php`, `.env.local` — there's no local-dev fallback upload server), and `hostinger/upload.php`'s `$ALLOWED_ORIGINS` allowlist only had bare `http://localhost:5173`. Browsers treat `spacekrafters-ops.localhost` and `localhost` as different hostnames/origins entirely, so the CORS preflight was being rejected for this specific dev host even though plain `localhost:5173` would've worked fine — a second, independent cause layered on top of the still-unresolved stale-deployment issue.
- **Changed:** `hostinger/upload.php` — added `'http://spacekrafters-ops.localhost:5173'` to `$ALLOWED_ORIGINS`.
- No database/schema change — this file isn't part of the Supabase schema or the Vercel git deploy.
- **⚠ DEPLOY — manually, outside my access:** this file lives on Hostinger, not Vercel/git, so this change (and the pre-existing `spacekrafters-os.vercel.app` entry from the Sep 18 fix, confirmed via live `curl -i -X OPTIONS` to still be missing from what's actually running) both need `hostinger/upload.php` re-uploaded to the live Hostinger server by whoever has FTP/SSH/hPanel access. Until that re-upload happens, uploads will keep failing both in production and on this specific local dev host — this code fix alone does not take effect live.
- **Verified:** read `hostinger/upload.php`, `src/utils/fileStorage.ts`, and `.env.local` directly to confirm the dev server really does hit the live Hostinger endpoint (no separate local upload path exists) and that the origin mismatch is exact-string-match, not a wildcard. No browser access this session to reproduce the failure directly.

### Fixed: app briefly landed on Home before correcting to Task on fresh load
**Commit:** Uncommitted
Root cause: the PWA's `start_url` is bare `"/"` (`vite.config.ts`), which has no route of its own in `App.tsx` — it fell through to the catch-all `path="*"` route, and that route (along with the `/login` route, for an already-logged-in visitor) hardcoded `Navigate to="/home"` regardless of role, instead of using `getDefaultRoute(role)` (the function that already correctly sends every non-owner role to `/my-tasks`, added earlier this session but apparently never wired into these two spots). So opening the app fresh — PWA launch, a bookmark, or anything landing on bare `/` — routed through `/home` first for a non-owner, before whatever got them to the Task tab corrected it.
- **Changed:** `src/App.tsx` — both the catch-all route and the `/login`-while-already-logged-in redirect now compute `defaultRoute` via `getDefaultRoute(user.role)`, same as `LoginScreen.tsx` already does after a fresh sign-in. Owner still lands on `/home` (its real default); every other role now goes straight to `/my-tasks` from a cold app open too, matching what already happens after logging in.
- No database/schema change.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (14) and `npm run build` all clean. No browser access this session to confirm the exact flash is gone — this fixes a real, confirmed-in-code bug in the right problem area (a hardcoded Home redirect on fresh app loads for non-owner roles); if any flicker remains after this, it'd point to something else (e.g. a service-worker reload timing quirk) worth a fresh report with it still happening.

### Fixed: New Project's "Client" dropdown rendered off-screen on mobile
**Commit:** Uncommitted
Root cause: Client was a native `<select>` with every saved client as an option (dozens, growing over time). A native select's open dropdown is positioned and rendered by the browser/OS itself, not by page CSS — so once the list is long enough, it can render past the edge of the screen with no way for the app to reposition, resize, or scroll it into view. This isn't fixable by adjusting alignment CSS on our side, since the app has no control over that popup at all.
- **Added:** `src/components/forms/SearchableSelect.tsx` — a reusable dropdown that opens as a normal, absolutely-positioned panel in the page's own DOM (scoped under the trigger, capped at a fixed max-height with its own scroll) instead of a native browser popup — so it scrolls naturally inside the BottomSheet like everything else on the page and can never render off-screen again. Includes a search box, since that's also the more useful way to find one client among dozens anyway.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Projects/ProjectsScreen.tsx` — New Project's Client field now uses it; "+ New Client" stays as a pinned option at the bottom. No other field on this page was touched — the other selects (Business Division/Category/Brand, etc.) have only a handful of fixed options and don't have this problem, so they're left as plain native selects.
- Found but did not touch: `src/components/forms/LocationAddressField.tsx` is a pre-existing component (from the original codebase, not added this session) implementing a very similar GPS-pin + Google Maps link pattern to what was just built fresh for the Lead location picker — worth knowing about for next time, flagging rather than retroactively swapping anything over.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (14) and `npm run build` all clean. No browser access this session — recommend reopening New Project on a narrow/mobile viewport and confirming the Client dropdown now stays fully on-screen and scrolls with the sheet.

### Fixed: raising a new MRF never notified QS at all
**Commit:** Uncommitted
Your report: "MRF sent to QS but not send any approval message." Root cause: `handleCreate()` (both the standalone `/mrf` screen's and a project's MRF tab's — a new MRF can be raised from either) saved the record and showed a local "Sent to QS" toast, but never actually called `sendNotification` — every later step (QS submitting, Lead Owner approving/rejecting, payment release) already notifies someone, but the very first step, raising the MRF, silently notified no one.

- No schema/database change — uses the same `sendNotification()` every other MRF step already calls.

### Attendance — MD/Admin can now Add or Edit a team member's attendance record
**Commit:** Uncommitted
Your ask: an Add and Edit button on the Attendance page. Investigated first: the underlying save logic (`markStatus`/`saveRecord` in `AttendanceScreen.tsx`) already accepted an arbitrary `targetUserId`, but no UI ever called it for anyone but yourself — MD/Admin had the team History table (read-only) but no way to create a missing day's record for someone else, or correct a wrong clock-in/out time on an existing one; only status (Present/Absent/Half Day) could be self-marked, and only for yourself.
- **Added:** an "Add" button next to "Download Team Sheet" (MD/Admin only) opening a form — pick an employee, a date, a status, and optionally In/Out time — that creates that person's record for that day. An "Edit" button on every row of the team History table opens the same form pre-filled (employee and date locked, since they're that row's identity) to correct the status, times, or absence reason.
- Both share one save path: the record id is always `att_<userId>_<dateKey>`, so Add and Edit are really the same upsert — there's no separate "does it already exist" branch to get out of sync. Editing never touches `startCapture`/`endCapture` (the GPS + stamped photo from a real Start/End tap) — those are only ever carried over unchanged, never fabricated by this form, per the existing "set once, never edited after" rule on that field.
- Self-service (the "My Attendance" Start/End buttons and Present/Absent/Half Day for your own day) is completely unchanged — this only adds a new MD/Admin-only management path alongside it, gated by the same `approve_leave` permission that already gates seeing the team History table at all.
- No schema/database change — same `sks_attendance` table and `upsertAttendance()` used everywhere else on this screen.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (14) and `npm run build` all clean. No browser access this session — recommend trying Add for a day with no existing record and Edit on one that has a real clock-in, to confirm both save correctly before relying on it.

### MRF approval step moved from Project Coordinator back to the project's actual Lead Owner, and rejections now return to QS for rework
**Commit:** Uncommitted
Your requested flow: MRF → QS → Lead Owner Approval (Approve/Reject) → Procurement, with rejections going back to QS with the reason, and full status history throughout. Investigated first: `MRFPanel.tsx` already had a `resolveLeadOwner()` helper and its own code comment noting MRF approval "used to be" Lead-Owner-based before an earlier change moved it to the Project Coordinator — and `approve_mrf` in `permissions.ts` was already granted to `project_coordinator`/`designer`/`qs` (exactly `ASSIGNABLE_LEAD_OWNER_ROLES`, the roles a Lead Owner can be), but the actual approve/reject gate in code checked `can('create_mrf')` (Project-Coordinator-only) instead — so the permission side was already set up for this, only the gate itself was wrong. Also found rejection was a dead end: it set status to `'rejected'` with no UI branch to act on it afterward — QS could see the reason in history but had no way to revise and resubmit.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/MRF/MRFPanel.tsx` — `canApprove` now checks the signed-in user is the project's real Lead Owner (`resolveLeadOwner()`, already computed in this file) rather than a role/permission alone; owner (MD) can still always act on their behalf. Added a "Rejected by Lead Owner" callout showing the reason, and extended the QS fill-in form to also open for `status: 'rejected'` (not just the first `pending_qs` pass) with a "Resubmit for Approval" button that sends it back to `pending_coordinator_approval` — same history-logging `saveTransition()` used everywhere else in this flow, so who/when/notes for every step (raise, submit, reject, resubmit, approve, procurement, payment) all stay in the one `mrf.history` audit trail, unchanged. Approval-pending notifications now go to the actual Lead Owner (falling back to a broadcast only if no lead is linked); rejection now notifies everyone who can act as QS.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/MyTasks/myTaskFeed.ts` — the personal Task tab's MRF item now routes `pending_coordinator_approval` to the specific Lead Owner (identity match) instead of every `project_coordinator`, and surfaces `rejected` MRFs to QS as "Revise & Resubmit MRF" (previously invisible there since nothing routed a dead-end status anywhere).
- No schema/database/table change — `MRFStatus`, `StatusHistoryItem`, and `resolveLeadOwner()` all already existed; this only changed who the approval gate resolves to and added one new reachable UI state (QS acting on `rejected`) using data that was already being saved.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (14) and `npm run build` all clean. Checked live (read-only) that all 3 currently open MRFs resolve to a real, active Lead Owner user through `project → lead → ownerId`, confirming the new identity-based gate has real data to match against — none of the 3 happened to be sitting in `pending_coordinator_approval` right now to click through live, so recommend walking one MRF through Reject → QS resubmit → Approve once you get a chance, to confirm the full loop end to end.

## 2026-09-25

### Fixed: New Lead form — selecting Type "FGD" didn't show the Brand dropdown
**Commit:** Uncommitted
Root cause: "Type" (`leadCodePrefix`, SK/FGD — just drives the lead-number prefix) and "Business Division" (a separate dropdown, 5 options including 'fgd') were never linked. The Brand dropdown was (correctly) gated on Business Division being `'fgd'`, but nothing ever set Business Division when someone picked Type "FGD" — so it stayed on "— Select —" and Brand never appeared unless the user separately noticed and picked "FGD — Future Group Distributors" a second time in the other dropdown. Confirmed live: 9 of the 13 leads already saved with Type "FGD" have the wrong Business Division (`sk_constructions`/`sk_interiors`) for exactly this reason — flagged below, not changed.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Leads/LeadsScreen.tsx` — new `onLeadCodePrefixChange()`: picking Type "FGD" now auto-sets Business Division to `'fgd'` (clearing Category, which FGD doesn't use); switching back to "SK" clears `'fgd'` back out. Business Division stays visible/editable as before (so the 9 already-mis-tagged leads can still be corrected by hand via Edit → Business Division → FGD). Brand is now validated as required whenever Business Division is `'fgd'`, in both the New and Edit lead forms.
- **Changed brand list, at your request** — `src/types/index.ts` (`BusinessBrand`), `src/data/businessDivisions.ts` (`BUSINESS_BRAND_OPTIONS`/`BUSINESS_BRAND_LABEL`): split the old bundled "Bosch, Siemens & Gaggenau" option into three separate brands (KitchenAid / Bosch / Siemens / Gaggenau, 4 total). Purely additive to the existing JSONB field — **no database/schema change**; the old bundled value is kept in the type/label map only so the one existing lead already saved with it (`FGD012`) still displays correctly, it's just no longer offered for new leads. This same `businessBrand` field is what `ProductSalesScreen`/`ProductSaleDetailScreen`/`QuotationsScreen`/`ProjectsScreen` already read to show the brand — no separate field needed on Quotation or Project to "carry it forward."
- **Data-quality issue found, not fixed — needs your approval:** 9 of 13 existing FGD-prefixed leads have the wrong `businessDivision` (`sk_constructions` ×6, `sk_interiors` ×3) from before this fix, so they never got a Brand and are miscategorized. Proposed fix (a data UPDATE, not a schema change): `UPDATE sks_leads SET data = jsonb_set(data, '{businessDivision}', '"fgd"') WHERE data->>'leadCodePrefix' = 'FGD' AND data->>'businessDivision' != 'fgd';` — affects lead IDs `FGD001`–`FGD003`, `FGD005`–`FGD011` (9 rows), only touches the `businessDivision` field, leaves everything else (name, phone, status, activity history) untouched. Brand would still need to be picked by hand afterward per lead since there's no way to infer which brand each one meant. Not run — awaiting your go-ahead.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (14) and `npm run build` all clean. Confirmed live (read-only query) which leads are affected by the pre-existing data issue, and that the fix is entirely additive/backward-compatible for the one legacy-brand record.

### New "Product Sales" module for FGD (KitchenAid / Bosch / Siemens / Gaggenau) — separate workflow, zero schema changes
**Commit:** Uncommitted
Client request: a lightweight, separate sales workflow for FGD appliance sales — Customer Enquiry → Quotation → Approval → Payment → Product Ordering → Delivery → Completed — kept apart from Interior Projects/Design/MRF/Procurement/Installation, reusing existing customer/brand/quotation/payment functionality. Planned via Plan Mode first (plan file `misty-squishing-hopcroft.md`), per the standing database-safety rule.
**No database schema, table, or RLS changes at all** — confirmed both statically (every table already stores its business data in a JSONB `data` column) and live: ran a disposable end-to-end insert/update/delete against the real DB (throwaway FGD lead → quotation walked through every real status to `client_approved` with an advance → a `type:'product_sale'` project shell → a payment milestone + receipt → stage walked to `completed` → all rows deleted) with zero FK/constraint errors. FGD/appliance scaffolding (`Lead.businessDivision:'fgd'`, `Lead.businessBrand`, the `'FGD'` lead-number prefix) already existed from before this session.
- **Added:** `src/types/index.ts` — `ProductSaleStage` (7-value: `customer_enquiry|quotation|approval|payment|product_ordering|delivery|completed`); `Project.type?: 'product_sale'` (absent = existing Interior project, fully back-compat), `Project.productSaleStage?`, `Project.pendingProductSaleStageRequest?` — all optional, all inside the existing JSONB `data` column. New `Permission` literals `view_product_sales`/`create_product_sale`/`approve_product_sale_stage`.
- **Added:** `src/screens/ProductSales/ProductSalesScreen.tsx` — list of FGD leads (`/product-sales`) with a derived/real stage badge per row; `ProductSaleDetailScreen.tsx` (`/product-sales/:leadId`) — one enquiry's Lead info, linked Quotation summary + deep-link into the existing Quotations screen (reused unmodified), "Start Product Sale" once client-approved, Payment Summary, and a real timeline merging Lead activity + Quotation history + the shell's own status history.
- **Added:** `src/screens/ProductSales/productSaleStage.ts` — pure `deriveProductSaleStage()`: Customer Enquiry/Quotation/Approval are read-derived from the Lead's linked Quotation status (no Project row exists yet); once a Project shell exists, its own `productSaleStage` is the source of truth.
- **Added:** `src/screens/ProductSales/createProductSaleShell.ts` — creates the minimal Project shell the moment a linked Quotation reaches `client_approved`, plus its `PaymentMilestone` (+ `Receipt` if the Quotation already recorded an advance) — the exact auto-milestone/receipt logic already used for Interior Projects (`ProjectsScreen.tsx`'s `commitSave`), reusing `upsertProject`/`upsertPaymentMilestone`/`addReceipt` unmodified.
- **Added:** `src/screens/ProductSales/ProductSaleStatusSheet.tsx` — a separate, simpler stage machine (`PRODUCT_SALE_STAGE_ORDER`/`ROLE_FOR_PRODUCT_SALE_STAGE`/`PRODUCT_SALE_STAGE_LABEL`) cloned from `ProjectStatusSheet.tsx`'s submit/approve/reject pattern, governing only the three real internal handoffs after the shell exists (Payment → Product Ordering → Delivery → Completed); Customer Enquiry/Quotation/Approval have no duplicate approval gate — they ride on the Quotation entity's own existing MD/client approval, unmodified.
- **Added:** `src/screens/ProductSales/ProductSaleEnquiryForm.tsx` — minimal enquiry intake (name/phone/email/city/brand/source/notes), creates a real `Lead` (`leadCodePrefix:'FGD'`, `businessDivision:'fgd'`) via the same `useCRM().addLead()` every other lead uses.
- **Changed:** `src/data/permissions.ts` — new permissions granted to `owner` (full), `project_coordinator`/`accounts`/`purchase` (scoped to their stage); new `SCREEN_PERMISSIONS` entries. `src/components/navigation/navItems.ts` — new "Product Sales" nav section (`/product-sales`). `src/App.tsx` — two new routes, same `ProtectedRoute`/`AppShell` pattern as every other screen.
- **Changed (keeping the two workflows apart):** `src/screens/Leads/LeadsScreen.tsx` — excludes `businessDivision === 'fgd'` from the Leads list (Product Sales has its own list/detail, never needs the Interior Lead screens). `src/screens/Projects/ProjectsScreen.tsx` — excludes `type === 'product_sale'` from the Projects list and from lead-to-project conversion eligibility. `src/screens/Dashboard/DashboardScreen.tsx` — excludes `type === 'product_sale'` from Interior KPI counts (active/new projects, Sales Team Performance) and from the Outstanding Payment breakdown; Receivables/Payables/Company Finance and the Dashboard's own revenue totals are **left unfiltered on purpose** — Accounts still needs one combined money view regardless of division.
- **Deliberate deviation from the original plan, found while reading the actual approval code:** the plan initially assumed filtering FGD quotations out of `MDTasksScreen.tsx`'s pending-approval queue and `/quotations` too. Reading `ApprovalSection.tsx`/`QuotationsScreen.tsx` showed that's the *only* place MD approval and client-decision recording actually happen for any quotation — filtering FGD out would have made an FGD quotation impossible to approve. Left `/quotations`, `MDTasksScreen.tsx`'s pending-quotes queue, and the `/approval` page completely untouched and unfiltered for FGD (already shows each quotation's brand/division there); `MDTasksScreen.tsx`'s project-stage-approval queue needed no change either way since product-sale shells never populate the Interior `pendingStageRequest` field they check.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (14) and `npm run build` all clean. Live-verified the complete data flow against the real Supabase DB with disposable rows created and deleted in this session (see above) — no FK/constraint errors at any step, JSONB round-trips exactly as written. Did not have browser/Playwright access in this session to click through the UI live; the screens themselves were built and type-checked but not interactively verified end-to-end in a running browser — recommend a quick manual click-through (create an enquiry → quotation → approve → start product sale → walk Payment/Ordering/Delivery/Completed) before relying on this in production.

The data to build a real per-user "what needs me next" feed already existed across `Lead.assignedRole/assignedToId`, `Project.assignedRole/pendingStageRequest`, `MaterialRequestForm.status`, `Task.assigneeId`, today's `Appointment`/`SiteVisit` assignments — none of it required a new table. No schema/table/RLS changes — pure UI + `SELECT`-only reads via services that already existed (`getAllTasks`, `getAllProjects`, `getAllMRFs`, etc.), per the standing database-safety rule.
- **Added:** `src/screens/MyTasks/myTaskFeed.ts` — pure function `buildMyTaskFeed()`, sources: project-stage-turn (role match, same as `ProjectStatusSheet`'s own gate), lead-turn (exact person match, mirrors `LeadsScreen`'s existing `assignedOnly` filter), MRF pending-for-my-role, my assigned project tasks, today's appointments/site visits I'm on, daily-report edit requests waiting on me as Lead Owner, and role-gated approval queues (leave for admin, expenses for accounts, purchase requests/orders for purchase) — each only included for the role(s) that actually hold that real permission elsewhere in the app.
- **Added:** `src/screens/MyTasks/MyTaskScreen.tsx` — structural sibling of `MDTasksScreen.tsx` (same hero banner/4 stat cards/card-list pattern), re-sourced to the logged-in user; adds an "Active Projects" section (coordinator, current stage-assignee, or has an open task there) with real completion % and a progress bar; Daily Tasks/Daily Reports surfaced as compact links into their own existing, untouched screens rather than rebuilt.
- **Added:** `src/screens/MyTasks/MyProjectDetailScreen.tsx` — a project detail/timeline page for non-MD roles, adapted from `OutstandingProjectDetailScreen.tsx`: same hero header/Call/WhatsApp/timeline, reuses the real `ProjectStatusSheet` unmodified for the actual submit-for-approval action (zero new approval logic), hides the Payment Summary section for field roles (`site_engineer`/`designer`), matching the exact `FIELD_ROLES` rule `ProjectWorkspaceScreen` already applies.
- **Added:** `src/utils/projectJourney.ts` — extracted `buildJourney()`/`JourneyEvent` out of `OutstandingProjectDetailScreen.tsx` verbatim (no logic change) so both project-detail pages share one real timeline builder instead of two copies that could drift.
- **Changed:** `src/types/index.ts`, `src/data/permissions.ts` — new `view_my_tasks` permission, granted to every role except `owner`; new `SCREEN_PERMISSIONS` entries for `my-tasks`/`my-tasks/project`.
- **Changed:** `src/components/navigation/navItems.ts` — new "Task" nav item (`/my-tasks`), gated on `view_my_tasks`. Since that permission and MD's own `view_md_tasks` are mutually exclusive, every user sees exactly one nav item called "Task" — never both, never neither.
- **Changed:** `src/App.tsx` — two new routes (`/my-tasks`, `/my-tasks/project/:projectId`), same `ProtectedRoute`/`AppShell` pattern as every other screen.
- **Bug found and fixed, not part of the plan:** `getDefaultRoute(role)` in `src/utils/permissions.ts` was dead code — `LoginScreen.tsx` always hardcoded `navigate('/home')` regardless of role, so the function (and its own unit test) never actually ran in production. Fixed by having `AuthContext.loginWithCredentials()` return the logged-in `role` in its result, and `LoginScreen.tsx` now calls `navigate(getDefaultRoute(result.role))`. Updated `permissions.test.ts` to match the simplified `getDefaultRoute` (`owner → /home`, everyone else → `/my-tasks`). Without this fix, no role would ever have actually landed on the new Task tab after login.
- **Pre-existing RLS limitation surfaced, not caused, by this build — flagged per the database-safety rule, not changed:** `sks_can_see_project()` (`supabase/rls-policies.sql`) restricts `site_engineer`/`designer` to only see a project if they already have a `sks_tasks` row assigned to them in it (or, for `project_coordinator`, only projects where they're literally the `owner_id`). This means the "Project — my stage's turn" feed item can be silently invisible to a site_engineer/designer for a project that just reached their stage but has no task assigned to them yet in it — a real gap in the existing RLS design, not something introduced here. If you want full coverage, the fix would be widening `sks_can_see_project()`'s `ELSE` branch to also allow `EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM sks_projects p WHERE p.id = p_project_id AND p.assigned_to_id = sks_my_id())` — I have not applied this; it needs your explicit approval per the standing rule.
- **Verified live, per role** (test accounts already in the DB from this session): accounts, admin, project_coordinator, site_engineer, designer, qs, purchase — every one now lands on `/my-tasks` after login (confirmed via the login-redirect fix), sidebar shows exactly one "Task" item. Spot-checked real, correctly-scoped feed content per role: project_coordinator (Raj Kamal) — 10 real items (7 leads at real statuses, an appointment today, a site visit today, a project task) plus 11 real Active Projects with correct stage/completion%; purchase (Titus) — an appointment, an MRF "Procurement Action" item, and a "Submit Procurement for Approval" project-stage item, all real; accounts (Manju) — a "Submit Reconciliation for Approval" project-stage item and a real lead; site_engineer (Santhosh) — 3 real leads at `Site Visit Scheduled` (correctly matching his role), 0 active projects (genuinely none currently visible to him, consistent with the RLS finding above). Clicking through to a project detail page rendered the real timeline/payment data for project_coordinator; confirmed "Project not found" (not a crash) when a site_engineer session was pointed at a project ID outside their RLS visibility. MD verified fully unaffected: still lands on `/home`, `/dashboard` and `/md-tasks` both work exactly as before, and MD is correctly blocked from the new `/my-tasks`. Zero console errors across every role tested. `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (14, including the corrected `getDefaultRoute` test) and `npm run build` all clean.

### New MD-only executive Dashboard
**Commit:** Uncommitted
Built from the two FENSTER reference screenshots as a visual/layout guide only — data and labels are SpaceKrafters' own throughout, per the instruction not to copy the reference app's labels/numbers. No dummy data anywhere: every card, chart and list reads from the app's real leads/projects/appointments/payment-milestones/receipts.
- **Added:** `src/screens/Dashboard/DashboardScreen.tsx` — the whole screen.
- **Access control (enforced at both the nav and the route, not just hidden UI):** new `view_dashboard` permission, granted only to `owner` (same MD-only pattern already used for `view_md_tasks`/`view_personal_finance`). **Changed:** `src/types/index.ts`, `src/data/permissions.ts` (added the permission + `dashboard: 'view_dashboard'` in `SCREEN_PERMISSIONS` — routes not listed there default-allow, so this entry is load-bearing, not decorative), `src/components/navigation/navItems.ts` (new "Dashboard" item, top of Main), `src/App.tsx` (new `/dashboard` route wrapped in the existing `ProtectedRoute`, which renders "You cannot open this page" for anyone without the permission — confirmed this blocks a direct URL visit, not just the sidebar link).
- **Filters:** All / Today / Yesterday / This Week / This Month / Custom (date range), driving the Revenue Overview chart's window. The "Today's ..." summary cards stay literally "today" regardless of the filter chip, matching the reference's own behavior (those cards are independently labeled "Today's X", not filter-scoped there either).
- **Revenue Overview:** real `Receipt` rows summed per day for the selected range, small hand-rolled inline-SVG area+line chart (no new charting library added for one chart) with the period total top-right.
- **Summary cards — reference → SpaceKrafters mapping (each is a fraction of a real, non-invented denominator; the reference's cards divided by a sales *target*, which doesn't exist anywhere in this app's data model, so none of these fabricate one):**
  - Today's Orders → **Today's Appointments**: count scheduled today, ring = completed-today / scheduled-today.
  - Today's Production → **Today's Projects**: count of projects *created* today, ring = new-today / active-projects-total.
  - Today's Installation → **kept**, mapped to `execution`-stage projects (the on-site build/installation phase in this app's own `PROJECT_STAGE_ORDER`) since there's no separate "Installation" concept — ring = execution-stage / active-projects-total.
  - Today's Collection → **kept**: real `Receipt` amount collected today, ring = today's collection / this-month's collection-to-date.
  - **Removed entirely, as instructed:** the "Open Mistakes" card — this app has no "Mistakes" module, nothing was copied over.
- **Sales Team Performance:** leads grouped by `ownerName`, bucketed Active/Negotiation/Production/Done. Active/Negotiation are pre-contract lead-status groups (genuinely ₹0 — no quotation-value join was added, so this isn't a placeholder, it's what the data actually has at that stage); Production/Done follow a `won` lead's linked Project (via `project.leadId`) — Production while the project's stage isn't `closed`, Done once it is, value = the project's real `contractValue`. `lost` leads are excluded (no bucket fits them). Expandable per person.
- **Outstanding Payment:** real `PaymentMilestone` rows where `amount > receivedAmount`, grouped by due month (matching Receivables' own outstanding definition, not a new calculation), expandable to per-project line items (project name, client, coordinator, Value/Paid/Due) — same data Receivables already shows, just rolled up.
- **Bug caught and fixed during verification:** the first pass computed "today"/date-range boundaries via `date.toISOString().slice(0,10)`, which converts to UTC first — since IST is UTC+5:30, this silently misfiled anything before ~5:30am local time onto the previous day (a receipt correctly dated "today" showed up under "today" as belonging to "yesterday" instead). Fixed by deriving date keys from local `getFullYear()/getMonth()/getDate()` instead of the UTC-based ISO string everywhere in this screen.
- **Verified live:** MD account (owner) — Dashboard nav item visible, `/dashboard` opens, all 4 summary cards/Revenue Overview/Sales Team Performance/Outstanding Payment render with real numbers, filter chips switch the revenue chart's range correctly (confirmed **Today** → ₹0/"No collections yet" vs **This Month** → ₹3,70,000 with the right chart, after the timezone fix), expanding a salesperson and an outstanding month show correct per-item detail, no "Mistake" text anywhere on the page. Non-MD account (site_engineer) — no "Dashboard" nav item, and visiting `/dashboard` directly by URL is blocked with the existing "You cannot open this page" screen, not the dashboard content. 13/13 automated checks passed, zero console errors. `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (14) and `npm run build` all clean.
- **Follow-up same day — card alignment brought in line with the reference:** removed the extra right-side icon each summary card had (the reference doesn't have one), recolored each card's big value to match its ring's accent color instead of plain dark text, made the label normal-case/bold instead of small uppercase gray, and added the small "This Month — September 2026" / "Today — 23 Sept 26" style caption line under the filter chips that the reference has and the first pass was missing. `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (14) and `npm run build` all clean; re-verified live via screenshot.
- **Second follow-up same day — page now fills the available width, and the Revenue Overview chart matches the reference's detail:** dropped the `max-w-5xl mx-auto` cap that was leaving a large empty gray gap on wide screens (no other screen in this app caps its width either). Chart: added faint horizontal grid lines, x-axis date labels under the line (first/middle/last point, e.g. "21 Sept … 22 Sept … 23 Sept"), and a real period-over-period trend badge next to the total (↑/↓ vs the immediately preceding period of the same length — e.g. this week vs last week — computed from actual receipts, not a fabricated comparison; hidden entirely when both periods are ₹0). `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (14) and `npm run build` all clean; re-verified live at a wide viewport via screenshot.
- **Fifth follow-up same day — Outstanding Payment now drills down into the project's real, existing detail page:** per the explicit instruction to reuse the existing project detail page rather than build a second one, and to not fabricate/merge stages that don't exist in this app's real workflow — inspected `ProjectWorkspaceScreen` (`/projects/:id`) first and found it already covers nearly everything asked for with real data: Overview tab has Current Stage + a real Stage History timeline (who/when/note per transition) + Project Details; Finance tab has Contract Value/Budget/Spend/Collections/Margin plus the full Payment Milestones list; Documents tab has grouped project files with existing View/Download; Team tab has assigned people. **Changed:** `src/screens/Dashboard/DashboardScreen.tsx` — each Outstanding Payment project row is now a button that navigates to `/projects/{id}?tab=finance` (that query param already worked — `ProjectWorkspaceScreen` reads it on mount), landing directly on the payment/milestone breakdown for that exact project; rows with no resolvable project (an orphaned milestone) stay inert instead of linking nowhere. This is the *same* page every other "open this project" link in the app already uses (MD Approvals, Procurement, etc.), so the data is guaranteed consistent everywhere, not a second copy that could drift.
  - **Explicitly not built:** a merged single timeline spanning "Lead Created" through "Completed" — that would splice the *lead's* own pre-conversion activity log (which does track Lead Created → Site Visit → Quotation → Sent to Client → Client Approved) onto the *project's* own post-conversion Stage History (Advance Payment → Designing → Execution → Procurement → Handover Prep → Final Payment → Reconciliation → Closed, this app's real 8 stages — not FENSTER's manufacturing-specific list). Building that merge wasn't asked for as a fallback if the existing page covers the core need, and doing it would mean inventing a combined structure this app doesn't actually have — happy to build it as an explicit next step if wanted, flagging it rather than deciding silently.

Confirmed the Overview tab of the same project shows its real Current Stage, Stage History and Project Details. Zero console errors. `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (14) and `npm run build` all clean.

- **Third follow-up same day — card typography, click-through detail, and a Leads Today banner:** each summary card's dark label went from `text-sm` to `text-base` (bigger) and its colored value went from `text-lg` to `text-base` (smaller). **Fourth follow-up same day:** per a closer look at the reference, the colored value needed to end up visibly *smaller* than the label, not equal — dropped it one more step to `text-sm`, so the label now reads as the more prominent line and the colored value sits smaller beneath it, matching the reference exactly. ### Persisted in-app notifications wired up — assigning a task/appointment now creates a notification the recipient sees when they next log in
**Commit:** Uncommitted  ·  **⚠ DEPLOY** (RLS policy — written, NOT yet applied to the live DB, see below)
Inspected first, as asked: `sendNotification()` (`src/utils/notifications.ts`) already existed as the app's one canonical alert entry point, called from 8 different screens — but it only ever fired OS push (requires the recipient's browser to already have push permission granted and be subscribed) and SMS/WhatsApp (still no-op, no provider configured). Neither is persisted anywhere, so a recipient who wasn't logged in / hadn't subscribed at the moment of assignment got nothing, and there was no in-app place to see it later — exactly the bug reported (assigned an appointment to Santhosh; nothing ever appeared in his account). Also found `sks_notifications` **already defined** in `schema.sql`/`rls-policies.sql` (id/user_id/read_at/data JSONB) and already live in the database — clearly designed for exactly this, but never wired to any service or UI. Built on top of that existing table rather than adding a new one.
- **Added:** `src/services/notificationService.ts` — `getMyNotifications()` (RLS already scopes SELECT to the signed-in user's own rows), `markNotificationRead(id)`, `createNotifications(userIds, title, body, url)`.
- **Changed:** `src/utils/notifications.ts` — `sendNotification()` now also calls `createNotifications()` alongside push/SMS, so every existing call site (appointments, reschedule requests, quotation/design/MRF/project-stage MD approvals, leave requests) starts persisting notifications automatically — no per-call-site changes needed, matching the "don't build a separate mechanism" instruction.
- **Added:** `src/components/layout/NotificationBell.tsx` — bell icon with an unread-count badge, in `AppHeader` (so it's on every screen that already uses it — confirmed 46 of the app's screens do; the one exception is the bare `HomeScreen` stub, which has no header/chrome of any kind today and wasn't changed). Fetches on mount, polls every 30s. Clicking a notification marks it read (`read_at` set) and navigates to its `url`; unread ones are visually distinct (bold + dot); only ever shows the signed-in user's own rows.
- **Changed:** `src/types/index.ts` — added `AppNotification`.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Appointments/AppointmentsScreen.tsx` — editing an appointment to add a new assignee now also notifies them (previously only appointment *creation* notified anyone; reassigning via Edit sent nothing).
- **The bug's actual root cause:** `sks_notifications` had SELECT and UPDATE RLS policies but **no INSERT policy at all** — so even before today, nothing could ever have written a row to it (default-deny). **Changed:** `supabase/rls-policies.sql` — added `authenticated insert notifications ... WITH CHECK (true)`, same "any signed-in user can write, only the owner can read" shape already used for `sks_site_visits`/`sks_document_versions`. **⚠ DEPLOY — I attempted to apply this directly to the live DB (same process used for RLS changes throughout this project's history) and it was blocked by this environment's own safety controls as a "modify shared resources" action; I did not find a workaround and did not try to bypass it.** Whoever manages the Supabase dashboard needs to run:
  ```sql
  CREATE POLICY "authenticated insert notifications" ON sks_notifications
    FOR INSERT TO authenticated WITH CHECK (true);
  ```
- **Verified live:** confirmed the bell renders and opens correctly for both an MD account and a regular employee (Santhosh/site_engineer) account, each seeing their own empty state. Assigned a real test appointment from MD to Santhosh: push/SMS both no-op as already known (unrelated, pre-existing), and the notification insert failed with exactly the predicted RLS error (`new row violates row-level security policy for table "sks_notifications"`) — confirms the diagnosis precisely, and confirms the app degrades gracefully (assignment itself still succeeds; the failure is only logged, nothing crashes). **Once the policy above is applied, no code change is needed for the feature to start working** — re-tested the same flow structurally (bell UI, click/mark-read/navigate logic) and it is ready to go. Test appointment deleted after (its lead was a real, pre-existing lead — left untouched). `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (14) and `npm run build` all clean.

### Qualified lead reschedule now always goes through MD approval — "Appointment Confirmed" self-serve button removed
**Commit:** Uncommitted
Inspected the existing reschedule/MD-approval system first, per the request to reuse it rather than build a separate mechanism — it already existed end to end (reason-required reschedule request, MD notification, "Review Reschedule Request" sheet with Accept/Reject on the MD Task page, permanent `rescheduleHistory` log) except for one gap: MD's Accept never advanced the lead's own status, so a Qualified lead had no way to reach Site Visit except the "Appointment Confirmed" button being removed — that self-serve bypass is exactly what this request asked to close.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Leads/LeadStatusSheet.tsx` — removed the "Appointment Confirmed" button and its `submitAppointmentConfirmed()` handler from the Qualified status popup. Only "Rescheduled" (which still requires a reason before submitting) and the sheet's existing generic "Cancel" remain. Updated the description text to explain MD approval is now the only path forward. `submitReschedule()` itself (reason requirement, request status, notifications, history) was not changed.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/MDTasks/MDTasksScreen.tsx` — `handleDecision()`: when the MD Accepts a reschedule request for a lead still at `qualified`, the lead's `status` is now also advanced to `site_visit_scheduled` (with the correct assigned role/person resolved the same way other stage transitions do), in the same `updateLead()` call that already logs the activity note. Reject is unchanged — lead status untouched, only the activity log and `rescheduleHistory`/appointment record as before. The advance is guarded to only fire for leads still at `qualified`, so it can't affect a lead already moved on some other way.
- **Verified live** on two disposable test leads (deleted afterward): confirmed the Qualified popup shows only Rescheduled + Cancel, no Appointment Confirmed. Reschedule → MD Accept: lead genuinely moved to Site Visit Scheduled, activity log recorded "MD accepted the appointment reschedule — site visit scheduled", persisted after a fresh re-login. Reschedule → MD Reject: lead stayed at Qualified, did not advance, activity log recorded "MD rejected the appointment reschedule" and the original reschedule reason. 9/9 functional checks passed (3 of the automated test script's own text-match assertions initially read as failures due to a case-sensitive substring search against text that's rendered uppercase by an existing CSS class — confirmed a test-script artifact, not a product issue, by dumping the actual DOM text). Zero console errors. `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (14) and `npm run build` all clean.

### Receivables: fixed the Final Payment / Record Receipt form (root cause was zero accounts, not a bug)
**Commit:** Uncommitted
**Root cause, found by inspecting the database directly:** `sks_company_accounts` exists and the fetch/dropdown code (`getAllCompanyAccounts()`, the `<select>`'s `.map()`) was already correct — the table simply had **zero rows**. No accounts meant the dropdown could only ever show the placeholder, and "Record Receipt" could never pass its own validation (amount + account both required) — which read as "the button doesn't work" even though the click handler and the entire save logic (update received amount, status, post to Company Accounts, allocate to the project, audit log, Receipt History) were already fully implemented and correct.

Once accounts exist, "Record Receipt" is now properly `disabled` until amount and account are both valid (previously always clickable, relying only on a post-click error toast).

- **Verified live:** confirmed the empty state (no accounts) shows the message + link, not a dropdown. Created a real account through the actual Company Accounts screen, confirmed it appeared in Receivables' dropdown. Confirmed the button stays disabled with only an amount, or only an account, and enables once both are valid. Recorded a receipt: received amount and status (Partial) updated correctly, Receipt History showed the account and reference used. **Fresh re-login** confirmed it all persisted. 9/9 checks passed, zero console errors. All test data (account, milestone, receipt, transaction, allocation, audit rows) deleted afterward — back to the real starting state of zero company accounts. `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (14) and `npm run build` clean.
- **Note for you:** the app currently has no Company Accounts configured at all — someone needs to add at least one real bank/cash account on the Company Accounts page before receipts can be recorded anywhere in the app, not just on this one milestone.

### Project stage changes now require MD approval (all 7 transitions)
**Commit:** Uncommitted
Confirmed scope first, since this is a real rework of a live system: applies to every stage transition (Advance Payment through Closed), not just closing — same pattern as MRF/Quotation/Design approval elsewhere in this app. The responsible role (per stage) now *submits* a stage-advance request instead of applying it directly; the project stays at its current stage until the MD approves or rejects.
- **Changed:** `src/types/index.ts` — `Project` gained `pendingStageRequest?: { toStage, note?, requestedBy, requestedById?, requestedByRole?, requestedAt }`; new `Permission` `approve_project_stage`.
- **Changed:** `src/data/permissions.ts` — `approve_project_stage` added to owner only (MD-only, matching Quotation/Design approval).
- **Changed:** `src/screens/ProjectWorkspace/ProjectStatusSheet.tsx` — each stage's action button now submits for approval (label changed to "Submit for MD Approval") instead of advancing immediately; nothing else about each stage's existing guidance/preconditions (procurement-complete check, punch-list count, outstanding milestones, etc.) was touched. While a request is pending: the submitter sees "waiting for MD approval"; the MD sees the request's details and **Approve** (advances the stage, notifies the submitter) / **Reject** (stage unchanged, requires a reason, notifies the submitter) buttons. Both decisions are logged in the project's existing Stage History — no new history mechanism, reuses what was already there. MD gets a push notification when a request comes in.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/MDTasks/MDTasksScreen.tsx` — pending stage requests now appear in "MD Approvals – Ready for Next Step" alongside Quotation/Designing/Rescheduled Appointment, same list, same card style; opening one navigates to that project where the Approve/Reject UI lives.
- Not changed: the `/approval` page (Quotation/Designing tabs only) — left as its own separate, established feature; project-stage approval surfaces via the Project page itself and the MD Task list instead.
- **Verified live on two disposable test projects (not real data):** submitted a stage request → confirmed the stage did **not** change yet → reopened Update Status as MD → saw the request + Approve/Reject → Approved → stage advanced, both history lines present, request cleared from the MD Task list. Separately: submitted another request → confirmed it appeared in "MD Approvals – Ready for Next Step" while pending → Rejected with a reason → stage stayed unchanged, rejection reason recorded in history. **Fresh re-login** on both confirmed everything persisted exactly as left. 11/11 checks passed, zero console errors. Test projects deleted. `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (14) and `npm run build` clean.
- **Note:** this changes real behavior for anyone currently mid-flow on a project (e.g. Accounts, Site Engineer, Purchase, Project Coordinator can no longer advance a stage on their own — every transition now needs MD sign-off first).

### MRF: each vendor now has its own separate file uploads
**Commit:** Uncommitted
Restructured the data model: vendors and files were two unlinked flat lists (`vendorNames: string[]`, `files: string[]`) — any file could visually look "shared" across all vendors since nothing tied a file to a specific one. Files now live inside the vendor they belong to.
- **Changed:** `src/types/index.ts` — new `MRFVendor { id, name, files }`; `MaterialRequestForm.vendors?: MRFVendor[]` replaces the old flat `vendorNames`/`files`, which are kept only as legacy fields for reading old records (never written by current code).
- **Changed:** `src/screens/MRF/MRFPanel.tsx` — added `migrateMrfVendors()`: reads `mrf.vendors` directly, or for a record saved before this change, synthesizes it from the old `vendorNames`/`files` (the one shared file goes under the first vendor, since the old data had no way to say which vendor it belonged to). The Vendors section now renders one block per vendor — name + its own "Upload File(s) for `<name>`" area — instead of a name list followed by one shared upload area. Adding a vendor creates a new empty block; removing a vendor deletes only its own files (via the existing file-delete path) and leaves every other vendor's `files` array untouched. The first save after this change (adding/removing a vendor, or changing its files) writes the record in the new shape and drops the legacy fields — this is exactly what completed the migration for the real in-progress MRF below.
- **Real data affected:** one in-progress MRF (project FRD-101) already had 2 vendors ("Haroon", "Mohamed") and 1 uploaded file under the old shared-list shape. Migrated the file under "Haroon" (the first vendor) since the old data didn't record which vendor it was for — move it to "Mohamed" instead if that's actually where it belongs, now that each vendor has its own upload area to do that with.
- **Verified live, three separate runs, checking the database after every step:** (1) a vendor pair with no prior migration needed, (2) a vendor pair seeded in the exact legacy shape, (3) the real MRF itself — each time: add a third vendor → its own upload area appears → upload a file to only that vendor → confirmed the file only appears in that vendor's block, not the others' → remove that vendor → confirmed only its own file was deleted and the other two vendors (with their own files) were completely untouched → reload the page → everything still correct. One earlier run did lose a vendor during this work; I could not reproduce it in three careful follow-up attempts and the code path doesn't show an ordering issue, so it looks like a one-off race in that specific run rather than a deterministic bug — I restored the real MRF's "Mohamed" vendor by hand afterward and it has since round-tripped cleanly three times. Flagging this rather than calling it fully closed. Zero console errors on every run. `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (14) and `npm run build` clean.

### MRF: notes now stay in the history, and approvers get notified when an MRF needs their approval
**Commit:** Uncommitted
Confirmed first: the approver stays the Project Coordinator (not MD/Owner) — "MD" in the request meant "whoever approves"; and Notes stays optional everywhere, focused on making sure entered notes are actually saved into history and visible there (not adding required-field validation).
- **Changed:** `src/screens/MRF/MRFPanel.tsx` — every step's Notes (raise, QS's Notes + Additional Notes, Project Coordinator's approval Notes) are now folded into that step's own History line (e.g. "QS submitted details and dates for approval — Notes: ... · Additional Notes: ..."), so they stay attached to that point in time instead of only showing the current/latest value. Procurement's status-update notes already had this (their own per-entry history) and are unchanged.
- **Added:** when QS submits an MRF for approval, everyone who can approve it (Project Coordinator role + Owner) gets a push notification ("MRF Pending Approval") with the project name, material/request details, who submitted it, Requesting Date, Expected Delivery Date, and Notes — tapping it opens `/mrf` to review before approving/rejecting. Uses the same `sendNotification` used elsewhere in the app (best-effort — never blocks the actual MRF update if it fails).
- **Verified live:** raised an MRF with a note → History immediately showed it; QS submitted with Notes + Additional Notes → both appeared in that History line, and the notification call fired (confirmed via network request) to both push and SMS/WhatsApp channels; approved with an approval note → History showed it too. The push/SMS calls fail locally with CORS (edge functions not reachable from `localhost`, same pre-existing limitation as other MD-notify features in this app) but did not block the MRF update — matches how this notification helper is designed. Zero console errors from the MRF code itself. Test MRF rows deleted; only the real "Plywood" MRF remains. `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (14) and `npm run build` clean.

### MRF: fixed a double-submit bug that could raise the same MRF twice
**Commit:** Uncommitted
Found while investigating: two near-identical MRF rows on project FRD-101 (both "Plywood", created 23ms apart) — a fast double-click on "Send to QS" was creating two rows instead of one, since nothing blocked a second click while the first save was still in flight. Confirmed with the user and deleted the duplicate (kept the original).
- **Changed:** `src/screens/MRF/MRFPanel.tsx`, `src/screens/MRF/MRFScreen.tsx` — every MRF save action (Send to QS / New MRF, Submit for Approval, Approve/Reject, Save Status) now guards against a second click firing before the first save finishes, and visibly disables the button while saving. The guard uses a `ref` rather than state, since two clicks that fire back-to-back can both run before React re-renders — a state-only guard (tried first) still let both clicks through.
- **Verified live:** a rapid double-click on "Send to QS" now creates exactly one MRF row (confirmed directly in the database, not just in the UI). Zero console errors. Test rows deleted; only the real "Plywood" MRF remains. `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (14) and `npm run build` clean.

### MRF workflow reordered: QS now sets both dates, Project Coordinator approves before Procurement
**Commit:** Uncommitted
Confirmed with the user first, since this changes an already-built workflow: QS (not Procurement) now sets Requesting Date & Expected Delivery Date, and approval now happens right after QS, done by the Project Coordinator (not the project's Lead Owner, and no longer the final step). New flow: **Project raises (optionally seeding Details/Notes) → QS fills/updates Details, Notes, Requesting Date, Expected Delivery Date → Project Coordinator approves/rejects → Procurement receives the completed MRF.**
- **Changed:** `src/types/index.ts` — `MRFStatus` is now `'pending_qs' | 'pending_coordinator_approval' | 'pending_procurement' | 'rejected'` (dropped `pending_lo_approval` / `approved`). `MaterialRequestForm` gained `notes` (QS notes) and `approvalNotes` (Project Coordinator's approve/reject notes); dropped `loId` / `loName` / `loNotes` (no more separate Lead-Owner resolution — the project_coordinator role and owner already gate this via existing permissions).
- **Changed:** `src/screens/MRF/MRFScreen.tsx` — the "New MRF" sheet (already reachable from the Project page's existing "Material Requests (MRF)" card) now has optional Material/Request Details + Notes text boxes, so Project can seed them. QS's step now also has the two date fields and requires all four before "Submit for Approval". A Project Coordinator (or owner) approval step (Approve/Reject + notes) replaced the old Lead-Owner approval, and now runs right after QS instead of after Procurement. Procurement no longer sets dates — once approved (`pending_procurement`) they just see the completed MRF and, as before, can log their own execution status updates.
- **Changed:** `supabase/schema.sql`, `supabase/rls-policies.sql` — updated the descriptive comments above `sks_mrfs` to match the new flow. No schema or RLS logic changed (status is a free-text column, and access is already role-based, not status-based) — **no migration, no deploy needed.**
- No changes to `src/screens/ProjectWorkspace/ProjectWorkspaceScreen.tsx` — the Project-page entry point ("Send to QS for MRF" button on the Overview tab, already navigating to `/mrf` with the project pre-filled) already satisfied the request.
- **Verified live:** from a project's Overview tab → "Send to QS for MRF" → filled Material Details + Notes on the new create form → created (`Pending QS`); opened it on `/mrf`, seeded Details/Notes appeared and were editable; filled Requesting Date + Expected Delivery Date, "Submit for Approval" → status `Pending Approval`; Approve/Reject shown with a Notes box; approved with a note → status `Sent to Procurement`, approval note visible, the old Procurement date-entry step is gone, and the Procurement execution-status section is now visible immediately; history log recorded both the QS and Coordinator steps correctly. Zero console errors. Test MRF rows deleted (also found and removed 2 leftover untracked test MRF rows from a prior session — `sks_mrfs` is now empty of test data). `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (14) and `npm run build` clean.

**Commit:** Uncommitted
**Cause:** History is read from each quotation's / design's own saved `statusHistory` (label "MD approved" / "MD rejected"), which only exists for decisions made after it was introduced. The two designs already approved before that ("Dinning Hall", "New Living room" — status `client_approved`) had no entry, so the Designing history was empty even though they were approved. (Quotation history was empty because all 8 quotations are still drafts — nothing approved yet.)
- **Changed:** `src/utils/approvalHistory.ts` — new `isApprovedBeforeHistory()` (status is past MD approval but there is no "MD approved" entry); `countMdDecisions()` now includes those, so the Task page "Done" figure matches the history.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/MDTasks/ApprovalHistory.tsx` — such records now appear as **Approved** rows. For designs, the approver and date/time are recovered from the saved activity log (`sks_audit_log`, `design_status_changed`); if none exists, it shows "MD" and the last-updated time. The expanded row says "Approved before approval history was recorded". Rows show **Approved / Rejected** (Accepted is kept for reschedules). Everything is read from saved DB records, never from UI state; Quotation history shows only quotations, Designing only designs.
- No migration, no edge function.
- **Verified live (Playwright):** Designing history now lists "Designing – New Living Room" and "Dinning Hall" as Approved (approver Test Owner, date from the activity log). Then full flow with new records: created a quotation + a design package → MD approved the quotation and rejected the design (with reason) on `/approval` → history rows appeared immediately → **page reload** → still there → **brand-new browser session, logged in again** → Quotation tab shows "Quotation – Mohamed · Approved" (Lead ID, Version, Amount, Approved by M. Nirmal Kumar (MD), date/time, Status) and the Designing tab shows the Rejected design (Package, Rejected by, date/time, Status, Reason) above the two older approved ones; Quotation history never showed designs and vice versa. Zero console errors. Test quotation, design package and audit rows deleted. `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (14) and `npm run build` clean.
- **Known limit:** history lives inside the quotation / design records, so deleting a lead (which deletes its quotations) also removes that quotation's history. A separate permanent approval-log table would prevent this — not built; awaiting a decision.

**Commit:** Uncommitted

### Quotation approval workflow enforced: Created → Pending MD Approval → MD Approve/Reject → Send to Client → Client decision
**Commit:** Uncommitted  ·  **⚠ DEPLOY** (RLS policy — already applied to the live DB, see below)
The Pending-MD → MD-approved → Send-to-Client → client-decision statuses and badges already existed (2026-09-17/18). Gaps closed:
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Quotations/QuotationsScreen.tsx`
  - New quotations are created directly as **Pending MD Approval** (`internal_review`) instead of Draft, and the MD(s) get a push/SMS/WhatsApp notification (`sendNotification`, best-effort). Draft is kept only for older quotations, which now show "Send to MD for Approval".
  - MD sees **MD Approve** and a new **Reject** button. Reject opens a sheet with a **required reason**; the quotation goes to `rejected`, the reason is kept (`rejectionReason`) and shown in a red "Rejected by MD" box. Send to Client is only offered once the status is MD Approved (unchanged) — it still uses the existing WhatsApp / Email choice. Non-MD users see "Waiting for MD approval".
  - **Approval History** section on the detail sheet: every action (created, MD approved, MD rejected + reason, sent to client via WhatsApp/Email, client approved / revision requested / rejected, edited & resubmitted) with **who (name + role) and date/time**, newest first. Stored in a new `statusHistory` array on the quotation (JSON — no migration; older quotations simply have none).
  - **Editing** a quotation that is MD Approved, Rejected or Revision Requested now sends it **back to Pending MD Approval** (logged, MD notified) — otherwise an approved quotation could be changed and sent to the client without the MD seeing the new version. Edits to Pending / Sent / Client Approved quotations do not change status (as before).
- **Changed:** `src/types/index.ts` — `QuotationStatusHistoryItem`, `Quotation.statusHistory`, `Quotation.rejectionReason`.
- **Changed (behavior):** `src/data/permissions.ts` — removed `approve_quotation_internal` from the **QS** role. MD Approve/Reject is now **owner-only**; QS can still create and edit quotations. (Previously QS could also "MD Approve".)
- **Changed:** `supabase/rls-policies.sql` — the `sks_quotations` UPDATE policy now also blocks QS / project coordinator from setting `internally_approved`, so approval is enforced in the database too, not just hidden in the UI. ⚠ DEPLOY — **already applied directly to the live database** (policy dropped/recreated in a transaction, verified via `pg_policies`).
- **Verified live (as MD):** create → list shows "Pending MD Approval", Send to Client absent; Reject disabled until a reason is typed → Rejected + reason box + history; Edit → back to Pending MD Approval; MD Approve → Send to Client appears; Send via WhatsApp → Sent to Client (history "via WhatsApp"); Client Approved; reload → status and full 6-entry history persisted. Zero console errors. Test quotation and its audit-log rows deleted. `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests, `npm run build` clean.
- Not tested live: the QS/coordinator side (only the owner test login exists) — that boundary rests on the UI permission change plus the RLS policy.

### Multi-file uploads: New Design Package files + consistent behavior across every upload field
**Commit:** Uncommitted
**Audit result.** The shared `MultiFileUploadField` already accepted several files at once and every record type already stores files as an array, so existing single-file data needs no migration. Every upload field in the app goes through it (Site Visits, Requirements, Quotations, Project Diary/Documents/Punch List, Daily Diary, Procurement bills, Expenses receipts/proof, Design). Gaps found and fixed below. Deliberately left single-file (not document uploads): profile photo (Settings), the live attendance camera capture, and the bank-statement CSV import (parses one file at a time).

Files become the package's **Version 1** ("Initial upload") so they share the existing version-history list with open/download. If saving the files fails after the package is created, the package is kept and the user is told to add them via "+ Add Version".
- **Changed (all upload fields):** `src/components/forms/MultiFileUploadField.tsx` — each file row now shows **name, file type (PDF/Image/Word/Excel/CSV…) and upload date/time** (read back from the timestamp our upload already puts in the stored filename — no stored metadata, works for files already saved); a multi-select where one file fails no longer throws away the ones that succeeded (they're added, and the failure is listed); new files always append, never replace. **Delete is permission-gated:** new permission `delete_uploaded_file` (owner + project coordinator); anyone can still remove a file *they just added* in the form they're filling in, before submitting.
- **Fixed:** six view-only file lists (Design version history, Project documents, Files, Requirements detail, Daily Diary detail, Expense proof of payment) passed a no-op `onChange` without `readOnly`, so they showed an Upload button and a Remove button that deleted the real file from the server without updating the record. All now `readOnly` (view + download only).
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Quotations/QuotationsScreen.tsx` — removed the one-file limit on the quotation document (label now "Quotation Documents (PDF)", still PDF-only); "Send to Client" and Design "Send to Client" now include links to **all** files instead of only the first.
- **Changed:** `src/utils/fileStorage.ts` (`getUploadedAt`, `getFileTypeLabel`), `src/types/index.ts` + `src/data/permissions.ts` (new permission).
- **Verified live (real uploads to the upload server):** picked 3 files at once on New Design Package (2 PDFs + 1 PNG) → all listed with type + upload time; removed one before submitting; added another → the earlier ones were kept; created the package; reloaded the page → all files present under Version 1; saved list is view/download only. Test package, version and the uploaded files deleted afterward (server deletes returned 200). `tsc --noEmit`, unit tests (11) and `npm run build` clean.

### Lead ID numbering made duplicate-proof (server-side, safe for simultaneous creates)
**Commit:** Uncommitted
The SK/FGD Type picker and auto-generated Lead ID already existed (required Type, read-only ID, separate SK/FGD counters, 3-digit padding). The weak spot was *how* the next number was picked: from each user's locally-loaded lead list, so two users creating at once (or a user with a stale list / limited row visibility) could get the same ID, with nothing in the database to stop it.
- **Added:** `supabase/schema.sql` — unique index `sks_leads_lead_number_uniq` on `data->>'leadNumber'` (the real duplicate guard) and function `sks_next_lead_number(p_prefix)` (SECURITY DEFINER, only accepts `SK`/`FGD`; returns highest existing + 1 across *all* leads, zero-padded; SK and FGD independent). **✅ Applied directly to the live database** (checked first: no existing duplicates), PostgREST schema cache reloaded, and confirmed `SK → SK003`, `FGD → FGD001` from the existing data.
- **Changed:** `src/services/leadService.ts` — new `getNextLeadNumber()` (calls the function) and `insertLead()` (plain insert, flags a Lead-ID unique violation as `duplicate`).
- **Changed:** `src/context/CRMContext.tsx` — `addLead` now assigns the Lead ID itself (the caller can no longer pass one): asks the DB for the next number, inserts, and if another user grabbed that number first the unique index rejects it and it fetches the next one and retries (up to 5 times). Also fixed a second same-instant collision: the internal record id `lead_<ms>` now has a random suffix, since two saves in the same millisecond were failing with a primary-key conflict.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Leads/LeadsScreen.tsx` — removed the client-side `nextLeadNumber()`; the "Lead ID: SKxxx (auto-generated)" preview under the Type picker now comes from the DB function; success message shows the assigned ID ("Lead SK005 created successfully!"). Type stays required (only SK/FGD offered) and there is no field to type/edit the ID.
- **Verified live**: creating without a Type is blocked; 5 SK + 2 FGD leads saved at the same instant from 7 separate sessions all succeeded as SK003–SK007 and FGD001–FGD002 — no gaps, no duplicates, no errors; whole table has no duplicate Lead IDs. Test leads deleted afterward. `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` clean.
- Note: numbering is "highest existing + 1", so deleting the newest lead frees its number for the next one (same as before). Leads created before numbering existed have no Lead ID and are unaffected.

### Fix: Site Visit "Visit Date" restricted to today onward (new visits only)
**Commit:** Uncommitted
User flagged that the New Site Visit date picker allowed past dates and asked for it to start from today. Note: an earlier audit (2026-09-17) had deliberately left this field unrestricted, reasoning that visits are logged after they happen (backdated is the normal case). Flagged that conflict back to the user via AskUserQuestion; they confirmed they want the restriction anyway, accepting that this now blocks logging a visit for a date before today.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/SiteVisits/SiteVisitsScreen.tsx` — added the same `todayStr()` + `min` + `onChange` clamp + submit-validation pattern already used elsewhere in the app (e.g. Daily Task, Procurement dates). Applied **create-only** (`!showEdit`): the Visit Date input gets `min={todayStr()}` and clamps typed/pasted past dates back to today, and `handleAddVisit()` rejects a past date with an inline error. Editing an existing site visit remains unrestricted, consistent with how other "created historically, edited later" fields are already handled in this app (Project Expected Handover, Follow-up Due Date) — an existing record may legitimately have a past date.
