### Feature: Appointment Confirmed / Rescheduled flow at the Qualified stage, with MD accept
**Commit:** Uncommitted
User no longer wanted "Confirm Site Visit Scheduled" to appear immediately once an appointment exists at the Qualified stage — wanted an explicit Appointment Confirmed / Rescheduled choice, with Rescheduled going through a reason → MD notification → MD Accept flow. Clarified one open design decision first (there's no existing Notifications/Tasks inbox screen in this app) — confirmed the MD review happens inline in the Lead's Update Status sheet, same place as today's screenshot, matching the existing MRF/Daily Report approval pattern rather than building a new inbox screen.
- **Changed:** `src/types/index.ts` — `Appointment` gains `rescheduleReason`, `rescheduledDate` (optional new proposed date), `rescheduleRequestStatus` (`'requested' | 'accepted'`), and requested/accepted by+at fields. Only the latest request's details live on the appointment — the running history lives in the lead's own `activity` log (reused, not a parallel history array), same convention already used for every other Lead-status transition.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Leads/LeadStatusSheet.tsx` — the `qualified` stage now: (1) shows **Appointment Confirmed** / **Rescheduled** buttons once an appointment exists (Appointment Confirmed does what "Confirm Site Visit Scheduled" used to — advances the lead to `site_visit_scheduled`; Site Visit is never reachable without it). (2) **Rescheduled** opens a reason textarea (required) + optional new date/time, sets the appointment to `status: 'rescheduled'` with `rescheduleRequestStatus: 'requested'`, sends a push/SMS/WhatsApp notification to every `owner`-role (MD) user via the existing `sendNotification` util with the lead name, Lead ID, contact, appointment details, reason, and the requested new date if given, and appends an activity-log entry to the lead ("Appointment rescheduled: {reason}"). (3) While a request is pending, the MD (owner role) sees a review card with all of the above plus who/when it was requested, and an **Accept** button; anyone else sees a read-only "waiting on MD review" message instead of being able to re-request. (4) Accepting sets `rescheduleRequestStatus: 'accepted'`, reverts the appointment to `status: 'scheduled'` (updated to the requested new date if one was given), and appends a second activity-log entry ("MD accepted the appointment reschedule") — the sheet then returns to the normal Appointment Confirmed/Rescheduled choice so the coordinator can proceed.
- No migration needed — both `Appointment` and `Lead` are JSONB-backed, purely additive fields.
- **Verified live end-to-end, not simulated**: created a real lead, qualified it, scheduled a real appointment, requested a reschedule with a reason — confirmed the two-button state, the reschedule form, and that the request landed correctly; reopened as the owner (MD) — confirmed the review card renders the lead name/ID, contact, appointment details, and reason exactly as specified; clicked Accept — confirmed the sheet returns to the normal two-button state and the lead's Activity Log shows both "Appointment rescheduled: ..." and "MD accepted the appointment reschedule" in order, persisted (reloaded the page and re-confirmed). Zero console errors. Test lead/appointment deleted afterward. `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean.

### UI: Date/date-time inputs now open the picker when clicked anywhere
**Commit:** Uncommitted
User flagged that date inputs only opened the calendar when the small calendar icon was clicked, not the rest of the field. Native `<input type="date">` only opens on the icon by default; the fix is the browser's `showPicker()` API called from the field's own `onClick`.
- **Changed:** added `onClick={e => e.currentTarget.showPicker?.()}` to all 24 `type="date"`/`type="datetime-local"` inputs across the app (Site Visits, Follow-ups, Receivables, Payables, Project Workspace diary, Daily Report, Gantt deadline, Daily Diary, Projects start/handover dates, Expense Request, Leave from/to, Attendance, Appointments, Daily Tasks, Lead reschedule date, Procurement, MRF requesting/delivery dates, Personal Commitments/Investments/Finance). `?.()` guards browsers without `showPicker` support (falls back to the old icon-only behavior there, no breakage). No other logic touched — `min`/`max`/`onChange`/validation on every field is unchanged.
- **Verified live**: clicked the text area (not the icon) of a date input — `showPicker()` fired with zero console errors. `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean.

### Feature: Procurement Status on MRF, Vendors/Labour Listing, Site Engineer Daily Report (locked + LO-approved edits)
**Commit:** Uncommitted
Three more client asks (22/23/24), implemented and verified live.

**22. Procurement Status Update (extends today's MRF)**
- **Added:** `src/data/procurementStatuses.ts` — `PROCUREMENT_STATUS_OPTIONS`, a plain configurable string array (placeholder defaults, client will share the real list — edit this one file to change it, same pattern as `businessDivisions.ts`/quotation `UNIT_OPTIONS`). `src/types/index.ts` — `MaterialRequestForm` gains `procurementStatus?: string` and `procurementStatusHistory?: ProcurementStatusHistoryItem[]` (status, `isDelay`, notes, updatedBy, updatedAt) — additive, no schema change (still inside the existing `sks_mrfs.data` JSONB).
- **Changed:** `src/screens/MRF/MRFScreen.tsx` — once an MRF is `approved`, a "Procurement Status" section appears (visible to everyone who can already see the MRF, including the LO) with an "Update" action for Procurement (`set_mrf_dates` permission, reused rather than adding a redundant new one). Update form: status dropdown + a "This update reports a delay" checkbox that makes Notes **required** only then — matches "if there is any delay, Procurement must enter the reason" without trying to guess which configurable status strings mean "delayed." Every update is appended to history, never overwrites it.
- **Verified live**: created and fully approved a real MRF, updated its status to "In Transit" with the delay checkbox + a reason — the summary box and the history log both show it correctly, with date/time and who updated it.

**23. Vendors / Labour Listing**
- **New table** (didn't fit the existing, heavier `Vendor` master which carries GSTIN/address/contact fields this simple roster doesn't need) — `supabase/schema.sql`/`rls-policies.sql`: `sks_vendor_labour`. **✅ Deployed directly to the live database** (same verified process as the rest of today's new tables — table, RLS, grants, schema-cache reload, confirmed via `information_schema`/`pg_policies`).
- **Added:** `src/types/index.ts` — `VendorLabourEntry` (name, natureOfWork, and a plain-string `number` field — "the exact format/options will be shared," so deliberately not a typed/constrained field). `view_vendors_labour` (owner, project_coordinator, qs, designer, site_engineer, accounts — exactly the "LO, MD, Site Engineer, QS, Accounts" the client listed, where LO = the roles that can actually be a Lead Owner) and `manage_vendors_labour` (owner, project_coordinator — who add/edit; the client only specified who can *view*, so editing was scoped to the same roles that manage other master data like Client Master, rather than guessing more broadly).
- **Added:** `src/services/vendorLabourService.ts`, `src/screens/VendorLabour/VendorLabourScreen.tsx` (new route `/vendors-labour`, nav item under Procurement).
- **Verified live**: added a real entry, appears correctly in the list with all three fields.

**24. Site Engineer Daily Report**
- **Deliberately a new entity, not an extension of the existing Daily Diary** — checked first: Daily Diary (`DailyDiaryEntry`) has no edit/update path in the code at all today (create-only), so retrofitting a lock + LO-approval-to-edit workflow onto it would have meant building that mechanism from scratch there anyway, with the added risk of changing behavior for whoever already uses Daily Diary for its current, simpler purpose. A separate `DailyReport` entity avoided touching that feature at all.
- **New table** (`supabase/schema.sql`/`rls-policies.sql`: `sks_daily_reports`, project-scoped). **✅ Deployed directly to the live database**, same verified process.
- **Added:** `src/types/index.ts` — `DailyReport` (date, totalLabourCount, natureOfWork, `areas: DailyReportAreaEntry[]` for per-area labour counts, notes, `locked`, `editRequestStatus`, `editRequestReason`, `editApprovalNotes`, `editHistory`). New permissions: `view_daily_reports` (owner, site_engineer, qs, project_coordinator, designer — "SE, LO, MD, QS"), `create_daily_report` (site_engineer), `approve_daily_report_edit` (owner + the LO-eligible roles).
- **Added:** `src/services/dailyReportService.ts`, `src/screens/DailyReport/DailyReportScreen.tsx` (new route `/daily-reports`, nav item under Projects & Tasks). Implements the exact flow requested: **Submit locks the report** (no direct edit path exists once `locked: true`) → SE can **Request Edit** with a required reason → the resolved LO (same per-project resolution as MRF: `project.leadId -> lead.ownerId`, owner can always act) sees a **Review Edit Request** action to Approve/Reject with notes → **approving unlocks the report for exactly one edit** → saving that edit **diffs the changed fields** (compares date/labour count/nature of work/area breakdown/notes against the pre-edit values) into a human-readable history note and **re-locks** it automatically. Every step appends to `editHistory` — submitted, edit_requested, edit_approved/rejected, edited — each with who and when, satisfying "maintain an edit/approval history showing who requested, who approved, date/time, and what was changed."
- **Verified live end-to-end, not simulated**: submitted a real report (18 labour, 2 areas) — confirmed **Locked**; requested an edit with a reason — confirmed **Edit Requested** badge; approved it as LO with notes — confirmed it flipped to **Editable**; edited the labour count (18 → 20) and saved — confirmed it **re-locked automatically**, the new value persisted, and the history log shows all four steps in order with the diff correctly reading "Changed: total labour count." Zero console errors across all three features. Test data deleted afterward. `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` succeeds.

### Feature: Project "Designing" stage (configurable order) + full MRF (Material Request Form) workflow
**Commit:** Uncommitted
Two related asks: add a Designing stage to the project pipeline with an order the client can still change later, and a brand-new multi-actor MRF flow (Project -> QS -> Procurement -> Lead Owner).

**16. Project Stages — Designing, kept reorderable**
- **Added:** `src/types/index.ts` — `ProjectStage` gains `'designing'`, inserted between `advance_payment` and `execution` (matches today's earlier Quotation-approval -> Advance Payment -> Design work — design logically starts once the advance is in and before site execution begins).
- **Changed:** `src/screens/ProjectWorkspace/ProjectStatusSheet.tsx` — extracted the stage sequence into one exported array, **`PROJECT_STAGE_ORDER`**, and a `nextProjectStage()` helper. Previously each stage's "move forward" button had its next-stage hardcoded inline (`save('execution', 'Execution started')`); now every transition reads its target from this one array, so **reordering stages later is a one-line array edit**, not a rewrite of the transition logic — directly matching "keep the stage order configurable/changeable." Added a new `'designing'` case with its own screen ("Design is in progress — track it in the Design Center tab... confirm once finalized").
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Projects/ProjectsScreen.tsx` (`STAGE_OPTIONS`, the manual stage picker) and `src/components/badges/StatusBadge.tsx` (shared label/color maps — safe to touch directly here, unlike the Quotation/Lead `internally_approved` collision from earlier, since `'designing'` doesn't collide with any other status anywhere in the codebase).
- Non-destructive: adding a stage value to an existing enum doesn't touch any project's already-stored `stage` — no existing project's data changes.
- **Verified live:** opened a real project's Edit form — stage dropdown now reads Advance Payment, **Designing**, Execution, Procurement, Handover Prep, Final Payment, Reconciliation, Closed, in that exact order.

**17 & 18. MRF (Material Request Form) — Project → QS → Procurement → Lead Owner approval**
- **New entity, new table** — this didn't fit any existing structure (Purchase Requests are ad-hoc, raised by anyone with `raise_purchase_request`, no QS/LO stages at all). **Added:** `supabase/schema.sql`/`rls-policies.sql` — `sks_mrfs` (project-scoped, JSONB `data`, same pattern as every other table here). **✅ Deployed directly to the live database this session** (table, index, RLS enable, all 4 policies, grants, and a PostgREST schema-cache reload — same verified process used for `sks_daily_tasks` earlier today) — confirmed via `information_schema`/`pg_policies` and a live REST call.
- **Added:** `src/types/index.ts` — `MRFStatus` (`pending_qs -> pending_procurement -> pending_lo_approval -> approved | rejected`), `MaterialRequestForm` (reuses the existing generic `StatusHistoryItem[]` for its audit trail rather than inventing a parallel history type). 5 new permissions (`view_mrf`, `create_mrf`, `submit_mrf`, `set_mrf_dates`, `approve_mrf`) granted per actor: owner has all; project_coordinator creates + can approve (as a possible LO); qs submits + can approve; purchase sets dates; designer can approve (also a possible LO) — mirrors `ASSIGNABLE_LEAD_OWNER_ROLES`, since "LO" is a specific *person* (the project's originating lead's owner), not a fixed role.
- **Added:** `src/services/mrfService.ts`, `src/screens/MRF/MRFScreen.tsx` (new route `/mrf`, new nav item under Procurement) — a dedicated screen so QS/Procurement/LO can each work their part of the queue across every project, the same pattern Quotations/Design Center already use. "Lead Owner" is auto-resolved per MRF at creation (`project.leadId -> lead.ownerId/ownerName`, falling back to the project coordinator if the lead has no owner on file) and locked in at that point — a later status action correctly shows "Waiting on {LO name}" to anyone who isn't that person or the owner.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/ProjectWorkspace/ProjectWorkspaceScreen.tsx` — new "Material Requests (MRF)" card in the Overview tab (per "In the Project section, add an MRF option"), showing this project's MRF count with a "Send to QS for MRF" button (deep-links to `/mrf` with the project pre-selected) and a "View MRFs →" link.
- **Found and fixed a real bug during testing**: the LO-approval gate initially only let the *exact* resolved LO act, with no owner override — inconsistent with every other approval gate in this app (`ProjectStatusSheet`/`LeadStatusSheet`'s `canAct` always lets `owner` act regardless of whose turn it technically is). Fixed before it shipped.
- **Verified live end-to-end, not simulated**, walking all four stages as a single owner account (who legitimately holds every permission in the chain): created an MRF for a real project (correctly auto-resolved "Raj Kamal" as Lead Owner) -> entered material details, submitted to Procurement -> set Requesting Date (25 Sept 2026) and Expected Delivery Date (2 Oct 2026), sent to LO -> entered LO notes and approved. Final state correctly shows status **Approved**, both dates, the material details, the LO notes, and a complete 4-entry timestamped history (Sent to QS → QS submitted → Procurement set dates → LO approved). Zero console errors. Test MRF deleted afterward. `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` succeeds.

### Feature: Advance Payment gate before Design + Design approval flow (mirrors Quotation)
**Commit:** Uncommitted
Client asked for: Quotation Approved → **Advance Payment Received** → Design (blocked until payment recorded), and Design to follow the exact same Created → MD Approval → Send to Client → Client Approval/Rejection flow just built for Quotations.

**Investigated the existing architecture first, since this touches live sales-pipeline data.** Found there are actually **two parallel status systems** in this app: `Lead.status` (a long, coarse pipeline covering the whole deal lifecycle, driven by `LeadStatusSheet.tsx` — "Update Status") and `Quotation.status` (the focused per-document approval pipeline built earlier today). Checked the live DB before touching anything: **8 real leads** currently sit in the exact statuses a Lead-pipeline reorder would touch (6 already `won` — closed deals with real Projects attached, 1 `client_approved`, 1 `sent_to_client`). Reordering `Lead.status`'s sequence to match the new diagram would have reinterpreted those leads' history. **Decided not to touch `Lead.status` or `LeadStatusSheet.tsx` at all** — instead built the actual enforcement at the one place "moving to Design" concretely happens: creating a design package in Design Center. This achieves the same outcome (you cannot get a new design started without the gate) without any risk to existing lead records.
- **Added:** `src/types/index.ts` — `Quotation` gains `advancePaymentReceived?: boolean`, `advancePaymentAmount?: number`, `advancePaymentRef?: string`, `advancePaymentAt?: string`. `DesignPackageStatus` extended (additive — old `in_progress`/`client_review`/`client_approved`/`revision_requested` values untouched for existing records) with `internal_review`, `internally_approved`, `sent_to_client`, `rejected` — the same shape as `QuotationStatus`.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Quotations/QuotationsScreen.tsx` — once a quotation is `client_approved`, a "Record Advance Payment" action appears (amount + reference, same pattern as the pre-existing Lead-level advance payment field). Once recorded, shows a green confirmation box and unlocks Design Center for that lead.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Design/DesignCenterScreen.tsx` — creating a **lead-linked** (pre-project) design package now checks `quotations.find(q => q.leadId === leadId && q.status === 'client_approved' && q.advancePaymentReceived)`; blocks with a clear inline warning (shown live as soon as the lead is picked) and a hard error on submit if it's missing. **Project-linked** packages are exempt — a Project can't exist without already having gone through the older, still-untouched advance-payment-at-Won step in `LeadStatusSheet.tsx`, so the gate is already satisfied by construction for that path. Replaced the old flat 4-button status selector with the same sequential flow as Quotations (Submit for MD Approval → MD Approve → Send Design to Client → Client Approved/Revision Requested/Rejected), reusing the existing `approve_design`/`request_revision` permissions rather than inventing new ones. "Send Design to Client" opens the same WhatsApp/mailto pattern as Quotations, linking the latest design version's file if one's been uploaded. Status labels use the same `StatusBadge` `label`-override mechanism from earlier today.
- **Verified live end-to-end, not simulated**: created a real quotation, approved it through MD Approval → Send to Client → Client Approved — **then tried creating a design package for that same lead before recording advance payment: correctly blocked**, both the inline warning and the submit-time error. Recorded the advance payment — confirmation box appeared. Retried creating the design package — **succeeded**, warning gone. Walked the full design flow: Created → Submit for MD Approval → MD Approve → Send Design to Client (captured the real opened WhatsApp URL, correct phone + message) → Sent to Client → Client Approved, final state correctly shows no further actions. Confirmed the 9 real pre-existing `in_progress` and 2 `client_approved` design packages still render correctly under the new labels (no regression). Zero console errors throughout. Test quotation and design package deleted afterward. `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` succeeds.

### Feature: Quotation discount type + PDF upload, MD-approval terminology, Send to Client via WhatsApp/Email
**Commit:** Uncommitted
Three related client asks (13/14/15) covering the same Quotation flow, implemented together and verified live end-to-end.

**Before writing any code, checked what already existed** — the status pipeline the client described (Quotation Created → MD Approval → Client Approval) was **already fully built**: `QuotationStatus` already has `draft → internal_review → internally_approved → sent_to_client → (client_approved | revision_requested | rejected)`, and "Send to Client" was already gated to only appear once `internally_approved`. So item 14 turned out to be a terminology/labeling gap, not a missing workflow — see below.

**13. Discount type (%/₹) + PDF upload**
- **Added:** `src/types/index.ts` — `Quotation` gains `discountType?: 'percentage' | 'amount'`, `discountValue?: number` (what the form edits), and `documentFiles?: string[]` (one supporting PDF). The existing `discountAmount` field is kept as-is and now always holds the *computed* final ₹ figure, so every existing consumer (`netAmount` math, Excel export, the print/PDF view) keeps working unchanged. All three fields are optional — pre-existing quotations have none of them and are treated as a flat ₹ discount equal to their existing `discountAmount`.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Quotations/QuotationsScreen.tsx` — `computeTotals()` now takes `discountType`/`discountValue` and derives the actual discount (₹ flat, or % of subtotal+tax) itself. The form's single "Discount Amount" field is now a type selector (Percentage/Amount) plus a value input, with the live total recalculating either way. Added a "Quotation Document (PDF)" upload (`MultiFileUploadField`, capped at 1 file, `accept="application/pdf"`) — saved with the quotation, shown read-only (view/download only, see the bug note below) in the detail sheet.

**14. Approval flow — MD-specific terminology**
- Didn't rename the underlying `internal_review`/`internally_approved` status values (would need a data migration and risks breaking anything reading them) or edit the shared status-label map (`internally_approved` is also a **Lead** status — renaming it there would've relabeled Leads too, which the client explicitly said not to affect). Instead: **added an optional `label` override prop to `StatusBadge`** (small, additive, every other call site untouched) and a quotation-only `QUOTATION_STATUS_LABEL` map, so quotations now show "Pending MD Approval" / "MD Approved" while Leads keep showing "Internally Approved" for the same underlying value, unaffected.
- Action buttons relabeled to match: "Submit for MD Approval", "MD Approve", "Client Approved", "Client Rejected".

**15. Send to Client — WhatsApp / Email**
- Clicking "Send to Client" (only enabled at `internally_approved`, per item 14) now opens a channel picker instead of transitioning immediately.
- **Technical reality check, not skipped:** browsers can't attach a binary file to a `wa.me` or `mailto:` link — those only support pre-filled text. **Added:** `handleSendToClient()` builds a message with the quotation's net amount and, if a PDF was uploaded (item 13), its real public URL (same Hostinger-hosted URL every other file in this app already uses), then opens `https://wa.me/91<phone>?text=…` or `mailto:<email>?subject=…&body=…`. Falls back to a clear error if the lead has no phone/email on file for that channel. Once opened, the quotation moves to `sent_to_client`.
- **Client Approve/Reject — asked first, since this had real security weight:** the client's wording ("the client should be able to Approve or Reject it… automatically reflect in the CRM") could mean either the client clicking a link themselves (a new *public, unauthenticated* page — this app has zero public surface today, so that's a first-of-its-kind addition needing a secure per-quotation token and a narrow anonymous-write policy) or staff recording the client's reply on their behalf (which already exists and already works — the `sent_to_client` stage's "Client Approved"/"Revision Requested"/"Client Rejected" buttons). **Client chose the latter** — no new work needed there, it already does exactly this.
- **Found and fixed a real bug while wiring the PDF viewer**, same root cause as the Site Visit Report entry earlier today: `MultiFileUploadField`'s "view mode" (no-op `onChange`) still had a working delete button that would silently destroy the real uploaded file. Applied the same `readOnly` prop fix (added earlier today) here too.

**Verified live end-to-end**, not simulated: created a real test quotation (10 sqft × ₹1,000, 10% discount) — form correctly computed Net Amount ₹9,000 live; uploaded a real PDF, confirmed it appears with view/download only (no remove) once saved. **10. Site Visit Details — MD Report**

New `'site-visit-report'` screen-permission entry, separate route guard.

Clicking it called `deleteRemoteFile()` against the real uploaded file regardless of the no-op `onChange` — the visible list wouldn't change, but the actual file would be gone from storage, silently breaking it for everyone. **Changed:** `src/components/forms/MultiFileUploadField.tsx` — new `readOnly` prop hides the Upload button and the remove button entirely (view/download still work). **Applied it to the 2 call sites this session was already touching** (Site Visits' own detail view, and the new Site Visit Report). **Not applied to the other 6 pre-existing call sites** (Daily Diary, Requirements, Project Workspace, Files, Expenses, Design Center) — same latent bug, but those are unrelated screens outside this session's scope; flagging clearly rather than silently rewriting 6 unrelated files. Worth a quick follow-up pass to add `readOnly` there too.
- **Verified live** against real existing site visit data (no test data needed for this one — it's read-only reporting): report list correctly showed real visits with accurate derived status, location, and room/photo counts; opened one with 2 real uploaded photos — full detail rendered correctly, remove button confirmed gone (only view/download), zero console errors. Confirmed owner can access the report and project_coordinator is correctly blocked with the standard "you do not have permission" screen, and doesn't see the nav item at all.

- **Changed:** `src/screens/Leads/LeadsScreen.tsx` — New Lead form (create only, hidden entirely in Edit — a lead's number never changes once assigned) has a required "Type" select (SK/FGD only) with the next number computed and shown live as soon as a type is picked (e.g. "Lead ID: SK004 (auto-generated)") — no text input exists for it anywhere, so it can't be typed or edited. Next number = highest existing number for that prefix + 1, padded to 3 digits, independent counters per prefix (computed from the currently-loaded lead list — no dedicated DB sequence table exists in this app for anything, so this matches the pragmatic approach used everywhere else here; noting the theoretical race condition on simultaneous creation by two people, acceptable at this app's scale).

### Feature/Fix: Tasks tab assignee visibility, Site Visit photo upload (CORS), Appointment/Site Visit separation
**Commit:** Uncommitted
Three separate client asks, investigated in parallel (3 research-only subagents) before any code changed, then implemented and verified live. Full detail in commit history; summary below.

**7. Tasks tab — tasks assigned to someone weren't showing up for them**
- **Root cause (two compounding bugs, not RLS):** (a) project tasks (`sks_tasks`) had **no assignee picker anywhere in the UI** — `ProjectWorkspaceScreen.tsx`'s "Add Task" and `TaskDetailScreen.tsx`'s "Add Subtask" both created tasks with `assigneeId` always unset, so no task could ever "belong" to anyone. (b) neither `TasksScreen.tsx` nor the project workspace's own Tasks tab did any user/role filtering — every visible project's full task list rendered to everyone identically, MD and employee alike.
- **Added:** an "Assign To *" required select to both Add Task and Add Subtask, plus a "Change" control on a task's own Assignee row (owner/PC only, via `edit_task`) — mirrors the pattern already used for Appointments' "Assign To" added earlier today.
- **Changed:** `topLevelTasks`/subtasks/`TasksScreen`'s per-project counts now filter to `assigneeId === user.id` for anyone without `edit_task` (site_engineer, designer); owner/project_coordinator (who have `edit_task`) still see and manage everything, unchanged.
- **Found and fixed a second, blocking bug while verifying live:** site_engineer/designer couldn't open a project *at all* to reach the Tasks tab — `/projects/:id` was gated behind `view_projects`, which only owner/project_coordinator hold. Raised this to the client rather than guessing, since the straightforward fix (grant `view_projects`) would also expose the Finance/BOQ/Procurement tabs' contract value, budget, margin, and payment milestones — completely unguarded once inside — to those two field roles. **Client chose:** grant project access, hide the sensitive tabs for those roles specifically. Implemented as a new `'project-workspace': 'view_tasks'` screen permission (looser than the `'projects'` list/`'clients'` screens, which stay `view_projects`-gated and untouched) — safe because every role that already has `view_projects` also already has `view_tasks`, so this only adds site_engineer/designer, nothing is removed for anyone. `ProjectWorkspaceScreen.tsx` now hides the Finance/BOQ/Procurement tabs (and blocks direct `?tab=finance`-style URL access to them) for those two roles only; the "← Projects" back button also correctly reads "← Tasks" and routes there instead of the still-gated `/projects` list for them.
- **Verified live end-to-end**, not simulated: logged in as owner, created one task assigned to a site_engineer test account and one assigned to a project_coordinator test account on the same real project. Confirmed: owner sees both (manager view). Logged in as the site_engineer account — could now open the project, saw **only** their own task (not the PC's), Finance/BOQ/Procurement tabs correctly absent from the tab bar, sidebar has no "Projects"/"Clients" item. Top-level Tasks nav correctly showed "1 open of 1 task" (their own count, not the project's real total of 2). Test tasks deleted afterward. `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` succeeds.

**8. Site Visit photo upload — not a code bug, a CORS misconfiguration**
- Investigated before assuming a missing feature: the upload UI/logic (`MultiFileUploadField` → `storeFile()` → Hostinger `upload.php`) was already fully built and is the exact same working code path used by Attendance, Daily Diary, Requirements, Expenses, and Punch List photos — Site Visits was never actually different or broken.
- **Confirmed the real cause by testing the live production site directly** (not guessing): logged into `https://spacekrafters-os.vercel.app` and attempted a real photo upload. Console showed: `Access to fetch at 'https://spacekrafters.com/os/upload.php' from origin 'https://spacekrafters-os.vercel.app' has been blocked by CORS policy... 'Access-Control-Allow-Origin' header has a value 'https://spacekrafters.com' that is not equal to the supplied origin.` — production's env vars are configured correctly; `hostinger/upload.php`'s `$ALLOWED_ORIGINS` allowlist simply never included the app's actual deployed domain.
- **Changed:** `hostinger/upload.php` — added `https://spacekrafters-os.vercel.app` to `$ALLOWED_ORIGINS` (kept the existing `https://spacekrafters.com` entry too, in case a custom domain gets pointed there later).
- **⚠ DEPLOY** — this PHP file lives on Hostinger (`https://spacekrafters.com/os/upload.php`), not Vercel/Supabase; I have no FTP/SSH credentials for it in this environment (same constraint noted when this file was first deployed, per the 2026-09-09 entry). **Someone with Hostinger access needs to re-upload the updated `hostinger/upload.php`, overwriting the existing file at that path.** Nothing else needs to change — confirmed by testing the exact same upload flow against local dev (which already has the real credentials in `.env.local`): upload succeeded end-to-end, file appeared with a live preview/download/remove UI, proving the client and server protocol are both already correct.
- Left one harmless orphaned test file on the Hostinger server from this verification (a 1×1 pixel test PNG, uploaded but never attached to a saved site visit — didn't chase deleting it, not worth the additional round trip for a throwaway file).

**9. Separate Appointment and Site Visit**
- Investigated structure before changing anything: tables, services, routes, nav items, and permissions were **already fully independent** — no DB foreign key between `sks_appointments`/`sks_site_visits`, separate services, separate routes/nav sections, separate permission sets. The only actual coupling was UI-level: `SiteVisitsScreen.tsx` fetched all Appointments data just to power an optional "Linked Appointment" dropdown in the New/Edit Site Visit form, plus an auto-search-and-link behavior when navigating from a Lead.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/SiteVisits/SiteVisitsScreen.tsx` — removed the "Linked Appointment" dropdown, its `getAllAppointments()` fetch/import, `handleAppointmentPick`, and the auto-link-a-scheduled-appointment behavior. Site Visits now schedules and records entirely on its own — Visit Date is a plain field the user sets directly, no longer auto-filled from an appointment.
- **Preserved the Lead → Appointment → Site Visit workflow**, per the client's explicit ask to keep it "where applicable": `LeadStatusSheet.tsx`'s stage gates (an appointment must exist before "Qualified" can progress; a site visit must exist before "Site Visit Scheduled" can progress) were already independent existence checks per module, not reliant on the two records being linked — left entirely untouched, still enforced.
- `SiteVisit.appointmentId` stays on the type (unused going forward, but not stripped from existing historical records — non-destructive).
- **Verified live:** New Site Visit form no longer shows "Linked Appointment" under any circumstance (checked both before and after picking a lead that has real scheduled appointments); Appointments screen was already one-directional and untouched. Zero console errors. `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` succeeds.

Mobile behavior (bottom-16, centered, 390px cap) is unchanged.

### Fix: Daily Task assignment failing — table existed, PostgREST just didn't know about it
**Commit:** Uncommitted
- Client reported Assign Task failing because `sks_daily_tasks` "does not exist." Investigated before touching anything: connected directly to the live DB (`SUPABASE_DB_URL`, same one-off `pg` script pattern used throughout this project's history) and found the table, its index, RLS, FK, all 4 policies, and all grants **already present and byte-for-byte matching** `schema.sql`/`rls-policies.sql` (compared column-by-column and grant-by-grant against `sks_weekly_plans`, a known-working sibling table — identical). So the 2026-09-17 `⚠ DEPLOY` SQL had in fact already been run against the database by someone — it just was never confirmed/logged as deployed.
- **Root cause:** despite the table being fully live in Postgres, `GET .../rest/v1/sks_daily_tasks` was still 404ing with a schema-cache error — PostgREST caches the DB schema and only reloads it on its own auto-detection trigger, which doesn't always fire for DDL run outside Supabase's dashboard SQL editor (or can silently miss a table). This is the exact "Could not find the table … in the schema cache" error the client saw; the frontend code was never the problem.
- **Fix applied directly:** sent `NOTIFY pgrst, 'reload schema'` over the same DB connection to force PostgREST to pick up the table. Verified fixed by calling the actual REST endpoint the app uses (`/rest/v1/sks_daily_tasks`) with the anon key — before showed the schema-cache error, after returns `200 []`.
- **No migration file needed** — `supabase/schema.sql` and `supabase/rls-policies.sql` already contained the correct `sks_daily_tasks` definition from 2026-09-17 (table, index, RLS enable, 4 policies); nothing new to add there. Confirmed `assign_daily_task` permission (owner-only, `src/data/permissions.ts`) lines up exactly with the RLS INSERT/DELETE policies' `sks_has_role('owner')` gate, and `updateDailyTaskStatus()` (real UPDATE, not upsert) lines up with the UPDATE policy allowing `assignee_id = sks_my_id()` — so an employee marking their own task Done won't hit the owner-only INSERT policy.
- **✅ DEPLOYED 2026-09-18** — table, index, RLS, all 4 policies confirmed live via `information_schema`/`pg_policies`/`pg_class`, and PostgREST schema cache confirmed reloaded via a live REST call. No frontend code changed this session, per instructions.
- **Not independently re-tested through the actual UI in a browser this session** (no running dev server / browser available here) — flagging so the client can do one real end-to-end pass: MD selects employee → enters Task/Notes/Date → Assign Task → appears under "Assigned So Far" → employee sees it in their own Daily Task tab → marks Done → survives refresh/re-login → MD sees all employees' tasks, employees see only their own. Every piece of this is backed by DB+RLS+permission checks verified above; the one thing not independently re-confirmed live is the browser click-path itself.

## 2026-09-17

### Change: Audited every date field in the app — restricted forward-looking ones to today+, left backdate-legitimate ones untouched
**Commit:** Uncommitted
- Client asked for the Daily Task past-date fix to be applied consistently everywhere it's relevant, explicitly calling out that genuinely historical fields (Attendance History, Leave History, Reports, etc.) must keep working with past dates. Used an Explore search across all 17 files with a `type="date"` input (20 fields total) and classified each by actual business purpose before touching anything — most needed no change at all.
- **Left unrestricted, deliberately (must allow past dates):** Attendance's "Mark Absent" date (backdated correction), Site Visits' Visit Date (visits are logged after they happen), Project/Daily Diary entry dates (recording work already done), Personal Finance transaction date, Personal Investments purchase date. Verified live that Attendance's Mark Absent and Site Visit dates still have no `min` restriction.
- **Restricted to today or later (added `min`, none had one before):**
  - `src/screens/Procurement/ProcurementScreen.tsx` — Purchase Request "Needed By" (create-only form, no edit mode reusing this field, so a plain `min={todayStr()}`).
  - `src/screens/Expenses/ExpenseRequestScreen.tsx` — Expense Request "Needed By" (same reasoning — create-only, the actual paid date is system-generated separately at approval time, this field is purely the requester's forward-looking target).
  - `src/screens/PersonalFinance/PersonalCommitmentsScreen.tsx` — "Expected Date" for `expected_income` commitments only (you can't "expect" income you already received). Left the main commitment "Due Date" *unrestricted* — on reflection that's more likely an anchor/setup date for an already-existing recurring bill/EMI/policy (same pattern as Personal Investments' Purchase Date), not a next-payment-due field, so restricting it risked breaking legitimate historical setup.
  - `src/screens/FollowUps/FollowUpsScreen.tsx` — Due Date restricted to today+ only when *creating* a new follow-up; editing an existing one (which can legitimately already be overdue — there's a real "Overdue" status/filter on this screen) keeps no restriction, mirroring the pattern below.
  - `src/screens/Projects/ProjectsScreen.tsx` — "Expected Handover" now gets the same conditional restriction Start Date already had (`min` = Start Date or today, but only when creating a brand-new project — editing an existing one stays unrestricted, since a project's actual handover may already be in the past).
  - `src/screens/ProjectWorkspace/GanttChart.tsx` — the "Edit Deadline" sheet's date input now has `min={todayStr()}` (a deadline is inherently forward-looking); doesn't force-clamp an already-past deadline value on an overdue task, so viewing/re-saving an overdue task's other fields isn't blocked.
- **Deliberately left as-is, flagging rather than guessing (genuinely ambiguous, and money-sensitive):** `src/screens/Finance/ReceivablesScreen.tsx` and `PayablesScreen.tsx`'s milestone/payable "Due Date" — could be a forward payment schedule (future-only) or a backdated/overdue correction to an existing record, and guessing wrong on financial due dates felt too risky to pick silently. Left unrestricted for now — let me know which behavior you want here and I'll apply it.
- None of these use a custom onChange-clamp beyond the Daily Task fix itself (which needed one because a manual/typed past date can bypass the native `min` on some browsers) — the rest rely on the standard HTML `min` attribute alone, matching how `Start Date`/`Leave From-To Date` already worked in this codebase before this change.
- **Verified live:** new Purchase Request/Expense Request "Needed By" both show `min` = today; a brand-new Project's Start Date and Expected Handover both show `min` = today; a new Follow-Up's Due Date shows `min` = today; Attendance's Mark Absent date and Site Visits' Visit Date both remain unrestricted (`min` = null) exactly as required. Zero console errors. `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` succeeds.

- **Added:** `supabase/rls-policies.sql` — 4 policies on `sks_daily_tasks`: SELECT (own or MD), INSERT (MD only), UPDATE (own — for flipping status — or MD), DELETE (MD only). **⚠ DEPLOY**, same reason as above.

- **Added:** `src/screens/DailyTasks/DailyTasksScreen.tsx` — MD's view lists every active team member (name + role, e.g. "Sakthivel – Quantity Surveyor") with a pending-task count and an Assign action; tapping a member opens a sheet to assign a new task (title/notes/date) and manage (remove) their existing ones. Everyone else's view is a flat list of only their own assigned tasks, each with a "Mark Done"/"Mark Pending" toggle — no title/notes editing, no visibility into anyone else's tasks (enforced by the RLS above, not just hidden in the UI).
- **Changed:** `src/data/permissions.ts` — `view_daily_tasks` added to every role's permission list, `assign_daily_task` added to owner only; new `SCREEN_PERMISSIONS['daily-tasks']` entry. `src/components/navigation/navItems.ts` — new "Daily Task" nav item under "Projects & Tasks". `src/App.tsx` — new `/daily-tasks` route.
- **Verified live:** nav tab renders for both an MD account and a regular employee account; MD's view shows the full team roster with Assign buttons and the exact permission-gated copy; the employee view correctly shows only "No Daily Tasks assigned to you yet" with no roster/assign controls visible. Attempting to actually assign a task against the not-yet-deployed table surfaces the real, specific Supabase error inline via the snackbar rather than crashing — confirms the UI degrades gracefully until the `⚠ DEPLOY` items above are applied. ### Feature: Automatic Absent / Half Day calculation, unified between the app and the Excel export
**Commit:** Uncommitted
- Follow-up to the Payable Days summary above — client confirmed two business rules needed to auto-calculate status rather than leave it manual: (1) a working day with no attendance captured at all should auto-show Absent, and (2) a Present day worked under a set number of hours should auto-downgrade to Half Day. Asked rather than guessed on both, since getting either wrong directly misstates someone's payable days:
  - **Working days = Monday–Saturday, Sunday = Week Off** — client confirmed this should match the existing Excel export's own convention (already pulled from their real reference spreadsheet: "Sunday = Week Off" banner in `attendanceExport.ts`), rather than introduce a second, different rule (Mon–Fri) just for the on-screen view.
  - **Half Day threshold = under 4 worked hours** (End − Start on a Present day).
  - Auto-Absent is bounded to never reach back before a person's first-ever real attendance record (there's no join-date field anywhere to check against instead) — so a day before someone ever started using the attendance system is never wrongly marked Absent — and never applies to today (still in progress) or a future day.
- **Added:** `src/utils/attendanceStatus.ts` — `computeAttendanceStatus()`, the one shared function that now decides Leave → Half-Day-by-duration → recorded status → auto-Absent, used by both the on-screen view and the Excel export so the two can never silently disagree.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Attendance/AttendanceScreen.tsx` — `effectiveStatus()` (used by the calendar, self history, the MD/Admin table, and the Payable Days summary from the entry above) now delegates to the shared function instead of its own simpler leave-only version. "My Attendance"'s status line and Present/Absent/Half Day button highlighting now reflect the same computed status (an auto-Absent day shows "(auto — no attendance captured)" so it's clear it wasn't a real record) instead of reading the raw stored status directly.
- **Changed:** `src/utils/attendanceExport.ts` — replaced its own separate `rec?.status ?? 'absent'` (which unconditionally marked *every* non-Sunday day of the exported month Absent, including days that hadn't happened yet, and never accounted for Half Day by duration) with the shared function. Side effect, not scope creep: exporting the current month mid-month no longer marks the rest of the month "Absent" before it's even happened, and auto-Absent is now correctly bounded per employee to on/after their first real attendance record.
- **Verified live:** confirmed no Sunday ever appears as an auto-Absent row; confirmed every currently-Present record under 4 worked hours in the real dataset now correctly displays "Half Day" in both the self view and the MD/Admin table; confirmed a manually-recorded future-dated test row still displays as-is (real data always wins over a computed guess); re-ran the Excel export end-to-end with no errors and a valid non-empty file. Did not fabricate a live gap-day scenario against real production attendance data to avoid leaving permanent unremovable test rows in a table with no delete path — the gap-day/auto-Absent branch is a straightforward extension of the same weekday/future-day guards already verified above. Zero console errors. `tsc --noEmit` clean.

### Change: Attendance Timing & Salary Impact — re-confirmed against existing GPS/photo feature, added Payable Days summary
**Commit:** Uncommitted
- Client re-sent the GPS/photo/timestamp attendance requirements this session already built (capture on Start/End, configurable office login/logout time, automatic Late/Early-by-exact-duration calculation, MD/Admin team history, no manual-edit UI path) — checked each point against the current code rather than re-implementing: all of it is already live (`AttendanceCaptureSheet.tsx`, `AttendanceSettingsScreen.tsx`, the Late/Early readout in `AttendanceScreen.tsx`'s self view and MD table).
- The one genuinely new, unambiguous ask was **"employees should be able to understand how their attendance affects their payable days/salary."** An earlier session already established with the client that there's no wage/salary field anywhere in this system, so no ₹ figure can be computed — this delivers the "payable days" half of that sentence without inventing a salary number.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Attendance/AttendanceScreen.tsx` — added a `monthSummaryFor()` helper and a "My Payable Days — {month}" card (shown to every employee, right below their own Start/End controls) totaling the days actually marked so far this month: Present counts as 1, Half Day as 0.5, Absent as 0, shown as "**X / Y days marked**". Deliberately only summarizes days that already have a real recorded status — doesn't assume a working-days calendar (weekends/company holidays aren't modeled anywhere in this app), so it never invents an "expected total" to compare against, only reports what's actually on record.
- **Two points from the request need a decision before they can be built, flagged rather than guessed:**
  1. *"Automatically calculate whether the employee is ... Absent"* — today, a day with no Start/End capture and no manual mark simply doesn't appear in history at all; it isn't auto-labeled Absent. Auto-marking it would need a working-days policy this app doesn't have yet (are Saturdays/Sundays working days? is there a company holiday calendar? what about days before an employee joined?) — guessing wrong here directly misstates someone's payable days, so this needs an actual answer rather than an assumption.
  2. *Automatic Half Day* — Half Day is still a manual mark (Present/Absent already are effectively automatic — Present is set the moment Start is captured). No threshold was given for when a day should count as Half Day on its own (e.g. arrived after a certain time, or worked fewer than N hours) — needs that rule specified before it can be automated.
- **Also re-flagging, since the request explicitly repeats "cannot simply enter or modify their own attendance time manually":** the app itself already has zero UI path to edit a captured `startTime`/`endTime`/`startCapture`/`endCapture` (Start/End only ever write through the automatic GPS+photo capture flow) — but the **DB-level trigger** that would block a direct Supabase API call from doing it (`sks_protect_attendance_capture()` in `supabase/rls-policies.sql`, written 2026-09-16) is still **⚠ DEPLOY**, not yet applied to the live database (self-applying schema/trigger changes is outside what's available to me in this environment). Real today, just not yet enforced at the database layer — whoever manages the Supabase dashboard needs to run that block.
- **Verified live:** the Payable Days card correctly shows "Present: 2, Half Day: 0, Absent: 0 → 2 / 2 days marked" against the real current month's data for the test account. Zero console errors. `tsc --noEmit` clean.

### Fix: Delete Lead (and every other confirm dialog) closed before the delete actually finished
**Commit:** Uncommitted
- Client reported deleting the "Rabbit New House Construction" lead didn't work. **Checked the live database directly first**: the lead is not present in `sks_leads` (32 rows, none matching) — the delete had in fact already gone through by the time this was investigated. Root cause found in `src/components/feedback/Dialog.tsx`'s confirm button: `onClick={() => { onConfirm(); onClose() }}` fired `onClose()` immediately, synchronously, without waiting for `onConfirm()` (an async function for every delete flow in the app — `deleteLead` cascades through `getAllQuotations()` + `getAllDesignPackages()` + per-quotation deletes + the lead row itself, several sequential network round trips) to actually finish. The dialog visually vanished the instant it was clicked, with no spinner or disabled state, so on a real network a multi-second cascade looked like a silent no-op — the delete was usually still completing correctly in the background, the user just had no way to tell and reported it as broken before it finished.
- This is a shared component used by every delete/destructive confirmation in the app (leads, quotations, projects, appointments, follow-ups, site visits, clients, vendors, users, personal finance, leave, etc. — 24 call sites), so all of them had the same silent-no-feedback issue, not just Delete Lead.
- **Changed:** `src/components/feedback/Dialog.tsx` — the confirm button now awaits `onConfirm()` (works whether the handler is sync or async) before calling `onClose()`, showing a spinner and disabling the Confirm/Cancel/X/backdrop-click while it's pending, so a slow delete now visibly shows "in progress" instead of appearing to do nothing. `onConfirm` prop type widened from `() => void` to `() => void | Promise<void>`.
- **Verified live**, via a throwaway Playwright test (removed after): created a real throwaway test lead, throttled the `DELETE /sks_leads` network response to 2.5s, confirmed the dialog stays open with a visible spinner ~200ms after clicking Confirm (previously would have already closed), closes only once the delete actually settles ~2.5s later, and the test lead is genuinely gone from the database afterward. Zero console errors. `tsc --noEmit` clean.

### Fix: "Open Camera" opened the Windows file picker instead of the actual camera
**Commit:** Uncommitted
- Client sent a screenshot showing that clicking "Open Camera" in the End Attendance flow opened the plain Windows file picker, not a camera. Root cause: "Open Camera" was a hidden `<input type="file" accept="image/*" capture="environment">` — the `capture` attribute is a mobile-only convention; desktop Chrome/Firefox/Edge ignore it entirely and silently fall back to a normal file picker, indistinguishable from "Upload Photo". Required behavior: **Open Camera → device camera opens → take photo → confirm → attendance submitted**, and **Upload Photo → file picker opens → select existing image → confirm → attendance submitted**, as two genuinely distinct flows, GPS/timestamp unchanged.
- **Changed:** `src/components/attendance/AttendanceCaptureSheet.tsx` — replaced the `capture="environment"` file input with a real live camera: `navigator.mediaDevices.getUserMedia({ video: { facingMode: 'environment' } })` feeding a `<video>` preview, plus a "Capture" shutter button that grabs the current frame onto an offscreen canvas and feeds it into the existing stamp/upload pipeline unchanged. Added `'camera'` (live preview + Capture/Cancel) and `'camera_error'` (permission denied or unsupported, with Try Again / Upload Photo fallback) stages to the sheet's state machine. Camera stream is stopped on capture, cancel, sheet close, and component unmount, so the camera light never stays on. "Upload Photo" is untouched — still a plain `<input type="file" accept="image/*">` with no `capture` attribute, opening the normal file/gallery picker. Also deduplicated the upload `<input>`, which had been accidentally duplicated across two conditional render branches, into one persistent hidden input.
- **Verified live** (throwaway Playwright with `--use-fake-device-for-media-stream`/`--use-fake-ui-for-media-stream` Chromium flags, removed after): clicking "Open Camera" renders a genuine live `<video>` element showing Chromium's synthetic camera test pattern (not a file picker), the video has real decoded frames (`readyState >= 2`, non-zero `videoWidth`), clicking "Capture" completes the full Start flow ("Started 02:28 pm", "Late by 4h 58m" correctly computed). Separately confirmed "Upload Photo" still completes a full Start independently via a real generated JPEG, with no `<video>` element ever created on that path. Zero console errors on either path. `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` succeeds.

### Feature: GPS-geofenced, photo-verified attendance (Start/End clock-in overhaul)
**Commit:** Uncommitted
- Client wanted to stop employees marking attendance from anywhere with a single tap: Start/End must now capture a photo and GPS position, reject the attempt outside a configured office radius, use the captured timestamp (not a typed one) for late/early calculation against configurable office hours, and be tamper-resistant after submission. Went through plan mode given the size (new device APIs, a new settings surface, DB-level immutability) — approved plan saved at the session's plan file.
- **Investigated first, since two parts of the ask assumed things that don't exist:** (1) no salary/wage field exists anywhere in this system (confirmed absent from `ManagedUser` and everywhere else) — asked the client, who confirmed showing hours/late/early/absent/half-day impact is enough, no ₹ figure to compute against. (2) confirmed `sks_integration_settings` (schema.sql) already exists, unused, with working RLS (SELECT any authenticated, INSERT/UPDATE owner-only) — reused as-is for the new office/geofence config instead of a new table.
- Asked the client one more implementation question: native camera picker (`<input capture>`, stamp the photo afterward via canvas) vs. a custom live in-app viewfinder — chose the native picker for reliability across devices/browsers, especially iOS PWA.
- **Added:** `src/utils/geofence.ts` — haversine distance + `isWithinGeofence()`.
- **Added:** `src/services/attendanceSettingsService.ts` — get/upsert the single office-config row in `sks_integration_settings` (`id: 'attendance'`).
- **Added:** `src/components/attendance/AttendanceCaptureSheet.tsx` — the actual capture flow: `navigator.geolocation.getCurrentPosition()` → geofence check (shows **"You are outside the permitted attendance location."** and stops before any camera step if outside) → native camera capture → stamps the photo with date/time/lat/lng via an offscreen `<canvas>` (GPS-camera-app look, matching the reference) → uploads via the existing `storeFile()` (`src/utils/fileStorage.ts`, already used for profile photos — no new upload path).
- **Changed:** `src/types/index.ts` — new `AttendanceCapture` (`lat`, `lng`, `accuracy?`, `photoUrl`) and `AttendanceSettings` types; `AttendanceRecord` gains `startCapture?`/`endCapture?`. Stored in the existing JSONB `data` column — no migration for the columns themselves.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Attendance/AttendanceScreen.tsx` — Start/End no longer write a record directly; both open `AttendanceCaptureSheet` and only write `startTime`/`startCapture` (or the `end*` equivalents) on its success — enforcing **GPS validation → photo → timestamp → record**, in that order. Both buttons are disabled with a clear message if the office hasn't been configured yet. Added a **Late by Xm** / **On time** / **Left early by Xm** readout (self view, self history, and the MD/Admin table's new **Late / Early** column) computed from the captured timestamp vs. configured office hours. Added a **Photo / GPS** column (MD table) and a "View photo & location" link (self history) opening a dialog with the stamped photo and a Google Maps link — reuses the *existing* history visibility split already built this session (MD sees everyone, employee sees only their own), just surfacing two more fields per row.
- **Added:** `src/screens/Settings/AttendanceSettingsScreen.tsx` (owner/admin only, `manage_settings` permission — new `SCREEN_PERMISSIONS['settings/attendance']` entry in `src/data/permissions.ts`) — office lat/lng (via a "Use my current location" button, stand at the office and tap it, or manual entry), geofence radius in meters, and office start/end time. Linked from `SettingsScreen.tsx` (new "Attendance" section, mirroring the existing "Manage Users" link) and from the Admin nav section (`src/components/navigation/navItems.ts`) plus a new route in `App.tsx`.
- **Changed:** `supabase/rls-policies.sql` — added `sks_protect_attendance_capture()` + a `BEFORE UPDATE` trigger on `sks_attendance`: once `startTime`/`startCapture` (or `endTime`/`endCapture`) are set, a regular employee can no longer rewrite them (owner/admin exempt, for genuine corrections). RLS row-conditions alone can't express "some JSONB sub-keys are now frozen," which is why this needs a real trigger. **⚠ DEPLOY** — writing/applying a trigger directly to the live database from this session is a "modify shared resources" action outside what's available to me here (same restriction hit earlier this session for an RLS change); whoever manages the Supabase dashboard needs to run this SQL block. Until then, the client never exposes any UI path to edit a captured photo/GPS/timestamp — real, immediate protection, just not yet tamper-proof against someone calling the Supabase API directly.
- **Verified live**, via a throwaway Playwright script (`playwright` + a real 480×360 test JPEG generated through an actual browser canvas — a 1×1 placeholder first missed the point, since there was nothing on it to see the stamp; regenerated properly, removed after): (1) office settings save real coordinates/radius/hours (owner-only nav item correctly gated — confirmed it also renders for the `admin`-role test account); (2) attempting Start from Delhi while the office is configured for Chennai shows the exact required rejection message and never reaches the camera step; (3) attempting Start/End from the exact configured coordinates succeeds, the resulting photo is visibly stamped with real date/time/lat/lng text in a bottom bar; (4) "Late by 3h 12m" / "Left early by 5h 58m" computed correctly against a 09:30–18:45 configured office day; (5) MD/Admin's history table shows the new columns and a working "View" link for records that have capture data, and correctly shows nothing for older pre-feature records rather than crashing. Zero console errors throughout. `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` succeeds.

### Feature: Delete Lead, available from every card, with safe cleanup of linked records
**Commit:** Uncommitted
- Client wanted a Delete option on every lead card/row (previously only reachable from inside a lead's full detail view), confirmed before deleting, and explicitly asked that related data (Project, Appointment, Site Visit, Requirements, BOQ, Quotation) never end up orphaned/broken regardless of the lead's stage.
- **Checked the actual DB relationships before writing anything** (`supabase/schema.sql`): Appointments, Follow-ups, Site Visits (and transitively Requirements, via Site Visit), and the lead's own activity log all already have `ON DELETE CASCADE` back to `sks_leads` — nothing to do there, they clean up automatically. Quotations (and BOQ sections/items under them) do **not** — deleting a lead that has a quotation would previously fail outright with a foreign-key violation, silently, since nothing surfaced that error clearly. Design Packages also reference a lead optionally but have no delete permission on that table at all.
- Asked the client what should happen to a Quotation tied to a lead being deleted, since blocking deletion would contradict their "must work regardless of stage" requirement — they chose: always delete it (and its BOQ) along with the lead.
- **Changed:** `src/context/CRMContext.tsx` — `deleteLead()` now fetches quotations and design packages first: deletes every quotation whose `leadId` matches (cascades to its BOQ sections/items automatically), and detaches (nulls `leadId` on, doesn't delete — there's no delete permission for that table, and unlike Quotation its `leadId` is optional) every design package whose `leadId` matches. Only then deletes the lead row itself, which cascades the rest. If any cleanup step fails, the lead delete is not attempted, so nothing is left half-cleaned.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Leads/LeadsScreen.tsx` — every lead card now has a "⋮" (more actions) button revealing a small menu with a red **Delete** option; confirms via the existing danger dialog ("Delete {name}? This also removes its linked appointments, follow-ups, site visits, requirements, and quotations.") before calling the same delete path already wired to the detail view's own Delete button (both now share one `deleteTarget` state instead of two separate flows). No other part of the card's layout/design changed.
- Iterated on the actual UI once live: first built it as a plain trash icon (per the initial ask), then switched to the three-dot "⋮" menu style per follow-up feedback. Building the click-outside-to-close behavior surfaced a real, non-obvious CSS bug worth flagging generally: this app's cards use `active:scale-[0.98] transition-transform` for a press effect — Tailwind's transform utilities emit a literal (identity, no-op-looking) `transform` CSS property the instant *any* scale/rotate/translate utility is used anywhere in a class list, even gated behind a pseudo-class variant like `active:`. That silently makes the card a CSS containing block, so a naive `position: fixed` element nested inside it (my first attempt at a click-outside overlay) only ever covers the card's own box, not the viewport — it looks fine visually but never actually closes on an outside click. Fixed by using a plain `document.addEventListener('click', …)` effect instead of a nested fixed overlay. Worth remembering for any future dropdown/popover nested inside a `scale-`/`rotate-`/`translate-` styled ancestor anywhere else in this app.
- **Verified live**, in order: (1) deleting a lead with no related records — confirms, deletes, snackbar shows "Lead deleted." (2) deleting a lead with a real linked Quotation (created via the actual Quotations screen, one section + one priced line item) — no foreign-key error, quotation disappears from the Quotations list, lead disappears from Leads, zero console errors. (3) three-dot menu opens/closes correctly, including closing on an outside click after the containing-block fix. All test leads/quotations created for verification were deleted afterward. `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` succeeds.

### Change: Lead Owner narrowed back to the 3 roles that actually work leads (QS, Designer, PC)
**Commit:** Uncommitted
- After broadening to all active team members (previous entry), user corrected this to only Sakthivel, Mohammed Fazil, Raj Kamal, Rinoshika. Asked whether that should be a fixed 4-person list or a role-based rule — they chose role-based, since it happens to be exactly `project_coordinator` + `qs` + `designer` today and should auto-include future hires in those roles.

- **Found and fixed a real, separate bug while verifying this:** Rinoshika didn't show up even though her `role` is `project_coordinator` — traced it to `getManagedUsersDirectory()` in `src/services/userService.ts:42`. The `sks_managed_users_directory()` RPC returns `roles: []` (empty array, not `null`) for the 2 accounts created without an explicit roles array (Rinoshika, Raymond Thirupugazh) — confirmed by capturing the actual RPC response over the network. `hasRole()`'s `user.roles ?? [user.role]` fallback only triggers on `null`/`undefined`, not on an empty array, so for these 2 accounts *every* `hasRole`/`hasPermission` check fed by this directory silently returned false regardless of their real role — this also means Raymond Thirupugazh (an MD) was very likely never being notified by this session's earlier leave-request push-notification feature (`LeaveScreen.tsx`'s `notifyApprovers()` also calls `hasPermission(m.roles ?? m.role, 'approve_leave')` off this same directory data), and any future screen that reads the directory and checks role/permission would hit the same silent failure for these 2 accounts.
- **Changed:** `src/services/userService.ts` — `getManagedUsersDirectory()` now normalizes an empty `roles` array to `undefined` (same as it already did for `null`), so the fallback to the single `role` field works correctly everywhere this directory is consumed.
- **Verified live:** captured the raw RPC response directly (confirmed `roles: []` for Rinoshika/Raymond before the fix); after the fix, Lead Owner dropdown shows exactly Sakthivel, Mohammed Fazil, Raj Kamal, and Rinoshika. Zero console errors. `tsc --noEmit` clean.

### Fix: Approved-leave highlight in the Attendance calendar landed on the wrong day entirely
**Commit:** Uncommitted
- User asked for approved leave dates to be clearly highlighted on the calendar (e.g. an approved leave for 17 Sep should highlight the 17th, in a color consistent with the "Leave" legend). The underlying logic for this (`effectiveStatus` → `isOnApprovedLeave`, already filtering to approved-only and handling multi-day ranges) already existed — but verifying it against Mohammed Fazil's real approved leave (17→17 Sep) showed the highlight landing on the **18th**, with the 17th cell showing nothing.
- **Root cause found and fixed:** `AttendanceScreen.tsx`'s `toDateKey(d)` built the date key via `d.toISOString().slice(0, 10)` — `toISOString()` converts to UTC first, which silently rolls the date back a day in any timezone ahead of UTC (IST included): a local midnight `Date` for "the 17th" becomes `2026-09-16T18:30Z`, so slicing reads back "16". That single helper is shared by every date-keyed thing on the screen — calendar cells, "today"/"selected" detection, and History rows — so this wasn't just a leave-highlight bug, it was silently shifting attendance dates by one day throughout the whole screen in IST. Rewrote it to build the key from local date parts (`getFullYear`/`getMonth`/`getDate`), matching the safe pattern `LeaveScreen.tsx`'s own `todayStr()` already uses.
- **Changed:** `STATUS_STYLE.leave` (`bg-slate-300 text-slate-800 border-slate-400`, up from a pale `bg-slate-100 text-slate-500`) so an approved-leave day reads as a clearly distinct, deliberately-colored cell rather than blending into the page's neutral gray tones — still the same slate family as the "Leave" legend dot, per the ask. Also changed the calendar's plain "today" indicator (when no status applies) from a `bg-slate-100` fill to bold text with no fill, since that fill was the exact same shade as the old Leave color and made the two indistinguishable whenever they were near each other.

### Feature: Real push notification to MD/Admin when a leave request is submitted
**Commit:** Uncommitted
- User asked for a notification when someone submits a leave request; asked whether they wanted an in-app badge or a real push alert — they said real push.
- **Found the push infrastructure was already half-built but silently broken:** `pushNotifications.ts`/`pushSubscriptionService.ts` and the Settings screen's "Push Notifications" toggle already existed, and `sw.ts` already had working `push`/`notificationclick` handlers — but `VITE_VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY` was never set (so the toggle was permanently a no-op), there was no send-side Edge Function at all, and `pushSubscriptionService.ts` wrote to columns (`managed_user_id`, `endpoint`, `p256dh`, `auth_key`) that don't exist on the live table — confirmed via direct schema introspection (`information_schema.columns`) that the real deployed `sks_push_subscriptions` is the standard `id`/`user_id`/`data` JSONB/`created_at` shape used by every other `sks_*` table, with 0 rows ever written (nobody could have used this before).
- **Changed:** `src/services/pushSubscriptionService.ts` — rewritten to match the actual live schema: `data` JSONB holds `{ endpoint, p256dh, auth }`, and the row `id` is a deterministic SHA-256 hash of the endpoint (so the same device upserts its own row on re-subscribe, while a second device for the same person gets its own row instead of overwriting the first). No RLS changes needed — the existing `user_id = sks_my_id()` policies already match this shape.
- **Added:** `src/utils/pushNotifications.ts` — `sendPushNotification(userIds, title, body, url)`, a thin wrapper calling the new `send-push` Edge Function. Fire-and-forget from the caller's perspective, same "best-effort" convention as `syncManagedUserAuth`.
- **Added:** `supabase/functions/send-push/index.ts` — Deno Edge Function (pattern matches `admin-user-sync`: verifies the caller's session, uses the service-role key to bypass RLS). Looks up subscriptions for the given `userIds`, sends via `npm:web-push` using VAPID keys from function secrets, and deletes any subscription that comes back 404/410 (expired/revoked) so it stops being retried forever. ⚠ DEPLOY — not yet deployed; needs `npx supabase login` + `supabase link` (an actual person's Supabase credentials, which I don't have) then `npx supabase functions deploy send-push`.
- **Changed:** `src/screens/Leave/LeaveScreen.tsx` — after a leave application saves successfully, `notifyApprovers()` finds every managed user with `approve_leave` (owner/admin roles) other than the submitter — via `getManagedUsersDirectory()` (already-existing `team` state if the submitter is themselves an approver, otherwise a fresh directory fetch, since a regular employee's `team` state isn't loaded) — and pushes "New Leave Request: {name} requested leave: {from} → {to}" to them, linking to `/leave`.
- **Generated VAPID keys this session** (`npx web-push generate-vapid-keys`) and set `VITE_VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY` in `.env.local` (gitignored) so local dev can actually subscribe; the private key is also recorded there as a plain `VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY` line (not `VITE_`-prefixed, so Vite never bundles it) purely as a record for whoever runs the deploy steps below.
- **⚠ DEPLOY — three things still needed before this actually delivers a push, in order:**
  1. `npx supabase login` (real person's credentials) + `npx supabase link`, then `npx supabase functions deploy send-push`.
  2. `npx supabase secrets set VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY=<value> VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY=<value>` (exact values are the ones just added to `.env.local` this session) plus `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` if not already set as a function secret from the `admin-user-sync` deploy.
  3. Add `VITE_VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY` (same public key) to Vercel's production env vars and redeploy the frontend — otherwise production keeps reporting push as unsupported exactly like it silently did before this session.
- **Also noticed but did not fix (out of scope for this task):** `sw.ts`'s push handler references `/icons/notification-bell.png`, which doesn't exist in `public/icons/` — a real push will still show (browsers fall back to a default icon), just without the intended bell icon.
- **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` succeeds. Since actually deploying the Edge Function requires Supabase CLI credentials I don't have, verified everything up to that boundary on a throwaway dev server (port 5199, so the user's own running dev sessions on 5173/5174 were left untouched) with `playwright`/`pg` installed via `npm install --no-save` (removed after): (1) the DB round-trip — upsert with the corrected shape, re-deriving the same id from the endpoint, delete — works exactly as the fixed `pushSubscriptionService.ts` implements it, verified directly against the live table; (2) submitting a real leave application (as a test account, deleted after) still shows "Leave application submitted!" and the app keeps working normally even though `notifyApprovers()`'s call to the not-yet-deployed `send-push` function fails (confirmed it fails via a CORS/network error in the console, not a thrown exception — the fire-and-forget call never blocks or breaks the leave flow). Real end-to-end push delivery (a notification actually arriving) can't be verified until the function above is deployed.
